Task 1: the shift label3Test 1 passes (2); a Record or one branch per shift, no default that hides a missing one (1).
Task 2: the list and its switch4Tests 2 and 3 pass (1 each); the shown list and the count derived from ONE piece of state, never stored (1); a FlatList with keys from volunteer.id and the empty message in ListEmptyComponent (1).
Task 3: add a volunteer3Tests 4 and 5 pass (1 each); the new volunteer added with a NEW array, and the field's message shown with accessibilityRole="alert" (1).

The solution's component:

import { useState } from 'react';
import { FlatList, Pressable, StyleSheet, Switch, Text, TextInput, View } from 'react-native';

import { shiftLabel, signedUp, type Volunteer } from './shifts';

export function SignUpList() {
  const [volunteers, setVolunteers] = useState<Volunteer[]>(signedUp);
  const [onlyRepair, setOnlyRepair] = useState(false);
  const [name, setName] = useState('');
  const [message, setMessage] = useState('');

  // Derived on every render, never stored
  const shown = onlyRepair ? volunteers.filter((v) => v.shift === 'repair') : volunteers;
  const count = shown.length === volunteers.length ? `${volunteers.length} volunteers` : `${shown.length} of ${volunteers.length} volunteers`;

  function add() {
    if (name.trim() === '') {
      setMessage('Type a name first.');
      return;
    }
    setVolunteers((current) => [...current, { id: `v${current.length + 1}`, name: name.trim(), shift: 'repair' }]);
    setName('');
    setMessage('');
  }

  const header = (
    <View style={styles.header}>
      <TextInput accessibilityLabel="Volunteer name" value={name} onChangeText={setName} style={styles.input} />
      {message !== '' && <Text accessibilityRole="alert">{message}</Text>}
      <Pressable accessibilityRole="button" onPress={add} style={styles.button}>
        <Text>Add volunteer</Text>
      </Pressable>
      <View style={styles.row}>
        <Text style={styles.grow}>Only repair tables</Text>
        <Switch accessibilityLabel="Only repair tables" value={onlyRepair} onValueChange={setOnlyRepair} />
      </View>
      <Text>{count}</Text>
    </View>
  );

  return (
    <FlatList
      data={shown}
      keyExtractor={(v) => v.id}
      renderItem={({ item }) => (
        <View style={styles.item}>
          <Text accessibilityRole="header">{item.name}</Text>
          <Text>{shiftLabel(item.shift)}</Text>
        </View>
      )}
      ListHeaderComponent={header}
      ListEmptyComponent={<Text>Nobody yet.</Text>}
    />
  );
}

Self-check: Part 1 Review

Q1
On a phone, what does a <View> become?
A) A <div> in a hidden browser
B) A picture drawn by Hermes
C) A native view, made by React Native's renderer
D) A WebView
Why: React Native renders to the platform's own views (Fabric); only the web preview turns a View into a <div> (Lesson 1.1).
A is wrong: There is no browser in the app.
B is wrong: Hermes runs JavaScript; it does not draw.
D is wrong: A WebView is a website inside an app.
Q2
tsc prints this for the project's ticket card. On a phone, what did the item name look like before anyone ran tsc?
src/components/TicketCard.tsx:29:77 - error TS2551: Property 'Item' does not exist on type 'Readonly<{ card: { gap: 8; padding: 16; borderWidth: number; borderColor: string; borderRadius: 12; backgroundColor: string; }; top: { flexDirection: "row"; alignItems: "center"; gap: 8; }; tag: { minWidth: number; alignItems: "center"; paddingVertical: number; borderRadius: 8; backgroundColor: string; }; t
Found 1 error in src/components/TicketCard.tsx:29
A) A red error screen
B) Plain text: the style is just undefined, so the name lost its look silently
C) Nothing: the card did not render
D) The app would not bundle
Why: Metro removes the types without checking them; an undefined style is ignored. Only npx tsc --noEmit names it, with a Did you mean (Lessons 1.2 and 2.1).
A is wrong: The code runs fine.
C is wrong: The card renders; only the style is missing.
D is wrong: Metro bundles without checking types.
Q3
A row of a tag badge and a long item name pushes the name past the right edge on a narrow phone. Which two flexbox settings fix it?
flexDirection: 'row' on the row (column is React Native's default) and flexShrink: 1 on the name, so it wraps
Why: React Native's flexbox starts in a column and items do not shrink by default (Lesson 2.2); the project's TicketCard does exactly this.
Q4
A screen draws under the notch and the home indicator. What does Shoreline Crew (and the Part 1 project) use?
A) Padding from useSafeAreaInsets(), here through useScreenScroll()
B) A fixed paddingTop: 44
C) <SafeAreaView> from react-native
D) Nothing: phones handle it
Why: The insets differ per phone and orientation; react-native-safe-area-context reports them (Lesson 2.3, then 3.1's useScreenScroll).
B is wrong: Right for one phone, wrong for the rest.
C is wrong: React Native's own is deprecated (and iOS-only).
D is wrong: They do not.
Q5
<Pressable onClick={toggle}>: what happens when a finger presses it?
A) A type error
B) It works like onPress
C) A red screen
D) Nothing, and no tool complains
Why: React Native's touch prop is onPress. onClick type-checks and lints clean and does nothing (Lessons 1.1 and 2.4).
A is wrong: tsc accepts it.
B is wrong: Nothing listens to it on a phone.
C is wrong: No error at all: that is the danger.
Q6
A "Show problem" button opens a panel. What must a screen reader hear besides the button's name, and how do you say it?
Whether it is open: accessibilityState={{ expanded: open }} (and aria-expanded for the web preview)
Why: State changes that sighted people see must be announced (Lessons 2.4 and 8.1); the project's C3 test checks it.
Q7
A teammate adds a short-name table and tsc prints this. What does it tell you?
src/utils/tickets.ts:5:14 - error TS2741: Property 'furniture' is missing in type '{ electronics: string; textiles: string; bikes: string; }' but required in type 'Record<Category, string>'.
Found 1 error in src/utils/tickets.ts:5
A) The union has a typo
B) A Record<Category, string> needs one entry per member: furniture is missing
C) Records cannot hold strings
D) Add as const
Why: A Record over a union is a complete table: tsc lists the missing keys (Lesson 2.5's ThemeColors works the same way).
A is wrong: The union is fine; the table is incomplete.
C is wrong: string is the value type given.
D is wrong: as const would not add the key.
Q8
Which way gives a style sheet dark-mode colors that follow the phone's setting?
A) StyleSheet.create({ card: { backgroundColor: lightColors.surface } }) at the bottom of the file
B) const { colors } = useTheme() at the top of the file
C) createThemedStyles(({ colors }) => StyleSheet.create({ ... })), then useStyles() in the component
D) An if in every style
Why: Both sheets are made once; the hook picks the current one on every render (Lesson 2.5). Colors for props come from useTheme() inside the component.
A is wrong: Worked out once: stays light.
B is wrong: Hooks only run inside components (the linter's rules-of-hooks).
D is wrong: Repetitive and easy to miss one.
Q9
ListHeaderComponent={() => <SearchField ... />}: what does a volunteer notice?
A) The keyboard closes after every letter
B) The app does not compile
C) Cards appear twice
D) Nothing
Why: A new function each render is a new component: React rebuilds the field. Pass an element (Lesson 3.1; Session 8 ran it headless: lo became l).
B is wrong: It compiles and lints.
C is wrong: No duplication.
D is wrong: The field loses what is typed.
Q10
Why keyExtractor={(ticket) => ticket.id} and not the index?
The key keeps each drawn row with its item; after filtering an index points at another item (an open panel jumps), an id stays
Why: Keys come from the data's identity (Lesson 3.1 measured the index-key bug).
Q11
What does FlashList do with a card that scrolls far off the top?
A) Unmounts it
B) Keeps it drawn
C) Reuses it for a card coming in, with new data
D) Caches its picture
Why: Recycling: rows go into a pool per item type and are drawn again (Lesson 3.2). State inside the row travels with it unless reset (useRecyclingState).
A is wrong: That is FlatList.
B is wrong: Only a small area around the screen is drawn.
D is wrong: It reuses components, not pictures.
Q12
Name the three rules that make infinite scroll safe to ship.
Ask once (only when idle), stop after a failure (only Try again asks), never show an item twice (drop known ids); stop at the API's X-Total-Pages
Why: Lesson 3.2; Lab 3.2's MSW counted the requests.

Item 2's and item 7's tsc output come from the project's model solution with the typo or the missing entry; with them undone, npx tsc --noEmit prints nothing.

Start Here · Part 1 Refresher

Not graded. Each reason names the Part 1 lesson to reread.

R1
A card shows {title} straight inside a <View>. What happens on a phone?
A) It renders like a span
B) A red screen: text must be inside a Text
C) tsc reports it
D) The title is skipped silently
Why: Every piece of text sits in a <Text>; tsc allows any child in a View, so only running it shows the error. Lesson: Native components (2.1).
R2
styles.tagLine is used, but the StyleSheet defines tagline. Who tells you?
A) The phone, with a red screen
B) Metro, while bundling
C) npx tsc --noEmit (TS2551, Did you mean)
D) Nobody can
Why: Metro removes the types without checking them and the phone ignores an undefined style; StyleSheet.create is typed, so tsc names it. Lesson: StyleSheet (2.1).
R3
Three Views inside a View with no flex styles. How are they laid out?
A) In a column, top to bottom
B) In a row, left to right
C) On top of each other
D) In a wrapping grid
Why: React Native's flexDirection defaults to column (the web's is row). Lesson: Flexbox (2.2).
R4
Which prop makes a <Pressable> react to a finger?
A) onClick
B) onTouch
C) onTap
D) onPress
Why: onClick type-checks and does nothing on a Pressable: React Native's touch prop is onPress. Lesson: Touch (2.4).
R5
In a FlashList, an open "details" panel shows up on another card after scrolling. Why?
A) The keyExtractor is missing
B) Recycling: useState in a recycled item keeps its value; use useRecyclingState
C) FlashList cannot show state
D) The list needs key={columns}
Why: FlashList reuses item components; state tied to the old item comes along unless it is reset per item (useRecyclingState). Lesson: Lists (3.2).
R6
A screen under a header bar keeps the 'top' edge. What does a notched iPhone show?
A) Nothing different
B) The header hidden behind the notch
C) An empty band of about 50 points above the content
D) A red screen
Why: The header already covers the status bar; keeping 'top' adds its height again. The web preview has no insets, so it cannot show this. Lesson: Safe areas (2.3).

4.1 · File-Based Routing: self-check

Q1
Which address does a file src/app/cleanups/index.tsx get?
A) /cleanups
B) /cleanups/index
C) /index/cleanups
D) None: index files are layouts
Why: index means "this folder's own address", like index.html on the web: src/app/index.tsx is /, src/app/cleanups/index.tsx is /cleanups (Lesson 4.1, Files Are Screens).
B is wrong: The word index never appears in an address.
C is wrong: The folder comes first, as in the path.
D is wrong: Layouts are _layout.tsx; index is a screen.
Q2
npx tsc --noEmit prints this in shoreline-crew. Which change fixes it?
src/components/EventCard.tsx:76:23 - error TS2820: Type '"/cleanup/[id]"' is not assignable to type '"/" | … | "/cleanups/[id]"'. Did you mean '"/cleanups/[id]"'?
Found 1 error in src/components/EventCard.tsx:76
A) Rename src/app/cleanups to src/app/cleanup
B) Write '/cleanups/[id]' in EventCard.tsx's Link
C) Edit .expo/types/router.d.ts to add the address
D) Cast the href with as Href
Why: Typed routes list every address the app has; '/cleanup/[id]' is not one, and TypeScript even suggests the right one. The kit ran it: the output is the lesson's card, word for word.
A is wrong: The folder is right: every other link and the screen itself use /cleanups.
C is wrong: router.d.ts is generated by Metro; never edit it.
D is wrong: A cast hides the bug: the volunteer would land on the not-found screen.
Q3
A Link passes params: { id: 8 }. What does useLocalSearchParams give the screen for id?
A) The number 8
B) undefined: params must be strings
C) The string '8'
D) ['8'], an array
Why: Params come from the address, and an address is text: you may pass a number when you link, but the screen always receives a string (Lesson 4.1, Params and Typed Routes).
A is wrong: The address turns it into text.
B is wrong: Numbers are allowed as input; they arrive as strings.
D is wrong: An array only comes from a repeated value or a catch-all route.
Q4
Why does the not-found screen's button use <Link href="/" replace> instead of a plain Link?
A) A plain Link cannot go to /
B) replace loads the list faster
C) replace is required inside +not-found.tsx
D) So back does not return to the dead end
Why: replace puts the list in the dead end's place. The course measured it: without replace, the browser's back button went straight back to /cleanup/e08.
A is wrong: Any Link can go to /.
B is wrong: Speed is the same.
C is wrong: Expo Router requires nothing of the kind.
Q5
The cleanup's title is only known once the API has answered. Where do you set the header's title?
A) <Stack.Screen options={{ title: event.title }} /> inside the screen
B) In <Stack screenOptions> in the root layout
C) <Stack.Screen name="cleanups/[id]"> in the layout
D) With navigation.setOptions from useNavigation()
Why: The third level of screen options: rendered inside a screen, Stack.Screen draws nothing and updates that screen's options from data. The layout gives the starting title "Cleanup".
B is wrong: screenOptions apply to every screen and cannot see this screen's data.
C is wrong: Good for what is known in advance ("Cleanup"), not for loaded data.
D is wrong: The older React Navigation way; in an Expo Router app use Stack.Screen.
Q6
You stopped Metro, added a route file, and now tsc says a link to it is not assignable to "/" | RelativePathString | ExternalPathString | "/_sitemap". What do you do before changing any code?
Start npx expo start: Metro rewrites the route types (.expo/types/router.d.ts), then run npx tsc --noEmit again
Why: The list of addresses is generated by Metro and refreshed while it runs; a stopped Metro leaves stale types. Nothing is wrong with the code (Lesson 4.1, Common Mistakes).

4.2 · Tabs, Nesting and Modals: self-check

Q7
What is the address of src/app/(tabs)/logbook.tsx?
A) /(tabs)/logbook only
B) /logbook
C) /tabs/logbook
D) It has none: files in a group are tabs, not routes
Why: A folder in parentheses is a group: it shares a layout and adds nothing to the address. Typed routes accept both forms; the course writes the short one.
A is wrong: Accepted by typed routes, but the address a volunteer sees is /logbook.
C is wrong: There is no folder called tabs.
D is wrong: Each file in the group is a route with an address.
Q8
Metro prints this after a change to (tabs)/_layout.tsx, and npx tsc --noEmit prints nothing. What does a volunteer see?
λ  WARN  [Layout children]: No route named "my-cleanup" exists in nested children: [ 'index', 'logbook', 'my-cleanups', 'profile' ]
A) A red error screen
B) Nothing different: it is only a warning
C) A tab bar without the My cleanups tab
D) A header titled "(tabs)"
Why: The course measured it: the tab with the misspelled name vanishes from the bar. Screen names are plain strings, not typed routes, so tsc is silent; the kit ran tsc on the same change and it printed nothing. The warning lists the names that exist.
A is wrong: Navigation mistakes rarely stop the app.
B is wrong: The bar loses a tab.
D is wrong: That comes from a root layout that still names "index".
Q9
Before you go has presentation: 'modal'. How does the web preview show it?
A) As a card sliding up from the bottom
B) As a half-height sheet
C) In a separate browser window
D) As an ordinary screen with a back arrow
Why: The course measured it: the web preview draws a modal (and a sheet) like any other stack screen. The card that slides up is a phone thing, described from the documentation; Done works the same everywhere.
A is wrong: That is an iPhone, per the documentation.
B is wrong: That is formSheet on a phone.
C is wrong: No new window is ever opened.
Q10
The Cleanups list has no header and sits above the tab bar. Which edges does it keep clear of the phone's insets?
A) ['top', 'left', 'right']
B) ['left', 'right']
C) ['bottom', 'left', 'right']
D) All four, the default
Why: Leave out every edge a bar already covers: the tab bar covers the bottom (it adds the home indicator's inset itself), nothing covers the top (Lesson 4.2's edges table).
B is wrong: That is a tab screen WITH a header (My cleanups, Logbook, Profile).
C is wrong: That is a screen under a stack header: a cleanup, the modal, not found.
D is wrong: The bottom inset would be added twice: an empty strip above the tab bar.
Q11
A screen is opened from several tabs and from links sent in messages. Where does its file go?
A) Inside one tab's own stack
B) As a fifth tab
C) In the root stack, next to (tabs)
D) In src/components, rendered by each tab
Why: One address, one place: in the root stack it opens on top of the whole tab navigator, and a link from outside (Lesson 4.4) always opens the same screen. That is where Shoreline Crew's cleanup screen lives.
A is wrong: Then it belongs to one tab, and other tabs would need copies.
B is wrong: Tabs are places a volunteer returns to all the time, three to five of them.
D is wrong: Only route files in src/app have addresses.
Q12
Why does Before you go's done() check router.canGoBack() instead of always calling router.back()?
Opened from its address (a link, the address bar) nothing is underneath, so back cannot go anywhere: it uses router.replace('/') then
Why: The same pattern as 4.1's leave(). Lab 4.2 Task 3 shows it: the starter's Done, opened at /tide-safety, stays on /tide-safety.

Lab 4.1 (guided): labs/module-10/l4_1-file-based-routing/starter → solution

As shipped, npm test (each failure's first line) and npm run typecheck:

● Task 1: Open on a site goes to /sites/<id> and shows that site
  Unable to find an element with text: Parking: Street parking on Cove Lane; arrive early.

  Unable to find an element with text: Calder Bay
● Task 2: an id that names no site says so
  Unable to find an element with text: No beach site has the id seal-rock.
● Task 2: opened from its address, Back to the sites puts the list in its place
  Unable to find an element with role: button, name: Back to the sites
● Task 2: opened from the list, Back to the sites goes back
  Unable to find an element with role: button, name: Back to the sites
● Task 3: a mistyped address gets the not-found screen, which replaces itself with the list
  Unable to find an element with role: header, name: This page is not on the map

The solution: npm test (npm run typecheck and npm run lint print nothing after their two > lines):

 PASS  src/routes.test.tsx
  ✓ Task 1: Open on a site goes to /sites/<id> and shows that site
  ✓ Task 1: the header shows the site's name
  ✓ Task 1: back returns to the list underneath, as it was
  ✓ Task 2: an id that names no site says so
  ✓ Task 2: opened from its address, Back to the sites puts the list in its place
  ✓ Task 2: opened from the list, Back to the sites goes back
  ✓ Task 3: a mistyped address gets the not-found screen, which replaces itself with the list
Tests:       7 passed, 7 total

What each task shows: (1) a route is where its file is: app/site/[id].tsx answers /site/…, so the list's /sites/… links hit Expo Router's Unmatched Route page; tsc finds only the untyped params (TS2345), never a file in the wrong folder (in the course app typed routes would at least have flagged a Link to an address no file has). (2) An id is outside input: a good string that names nothing; leave() is back-or-replace, and the tests open the screen both ways and check router.canGoBack() is false afterwards. (3) +not-found.tsx with a replace Link; the layout already titled it "Not found". Common sticking points: id as string; renaming the folder in the Link instead of moving the file; router.back() alone.

The solution's site screen:

import { router, Stack, useLocalSearchParams } from 'expo-router';
import { Pressable, StyleSheet, Text, View } from 'react-native';

import { findSite } from '../../src/sites';

// One beach on a screen of its own: /sites/driftwood-cove shows the site with the id driftwood-cove.
// (Shoreline Crew names the route instead, useLocalSearchParams<'/cleanups/[id]'>(), because Metro writes
// its typed routes; a lab has no Metro running, so the params' shape is written out.)
export default function SiteScreen() {
  // The [id] part of the address: always a string, it comes from the address
  const { id } = useLocalSearchParams<{ id: string }>();
  const site = findSite(id);

  // Back where the volunteer came from; opened from a link, there may be nothing to go back to
  function leave() {
    if (router.canGoBack()) {
      router.back();
    } else {
      router.replace('/');
    }
  }

  if (!site) {
    return (
      <View style={styles.screen}>
        <Text>No beach site has the id {id}.</Text>
        <BackButton onPress={leave} />
      </View>
    );
  }

  return (
    <View style={styles.screen}>
      {/* Options for this screen, set from inside it: the header shows the site's name */}
      <Stack.Screen options={{ title: site.name }} />
      <Text>{site.town}</Text>
      <Text>{site.description}</Text>
      <Text>Parking: {site.parking}</Text>
    </View>
  );
}
Lab 4.2 (guided): labs/module-10/l4_2-tabs-nesting-and-modals/starter → solution

As shipped, npm test (each failure's first line; typecheck and lint are clean):

● Task 1: the bar has three tabs, in order
  expect(received).toEqual(expected) // deep equality
● Task 1: the Saved tab opens its screen at /saved
  Unable to find an element with role: button, name: Saved, tab, 2 of 3
● Task 2: no header titled "(tabs)" above the tabs, only the tab header
  expect(received).toEqual(expected) // deep equality
● Task 2: a site opens on top of the tabs, and its back button says Back
  expect(received).toEqual(expected) // deep equality
● Task 3: opened from its address, Done puts the Sites tab in its place
  expect(received).toHavePathname(expected)

The warning in the starter's output, the one Metro would print:

[Layout children]: No route named "save" exists in nested children: [ 'guide', 'index', 'saved' ]

The solution:

 PASS  src/navigation.test.tsx
  ✓ Task 1: the bar has three tabs, in order
  ✓ Task 1: the Saved tab opens its screen at /saved
  ✓ Task 2: no header titled "(tabs)" above the tabs, only the tab header
  ✓ Task 2: a site opens on top of the tabs, and its back button says Back
  ✓ Task 3: opened from the Guide tab, Done goes back to the Guide tab
  ✓ Task 3: opened from its address, Done puts the Sites tab in its place
Tests:       6 passed, 6 total

What each task shows: (1) a Tabs.Screen name is the file's name without .tsx; the misspelled tab vanishes and the warning lists the names that exist (the fix is the layout, not renaming saved.tsx). (2) The root stack's children are now (tabs), sites/[id] and tide-safety: name="(tabs)" with headerShown: false, and headerBackTitle: 'Back' in screenOptions (the test reads the header bars react-native-screens would hand to the phone: titles, hidden, back words). (3) done() is back-or-replace. The stretch's edges: the Sites tab ['top', 'left', 'right'], Saved and Guide ['left', 'right'], a site and the modal ['bottom', 'left', 'right'].

The solution's root layout:

import { Stack } from 'expo-router';

// The root stack: the tabs are ONE screen of it (with their own headers), a site opens on top of them,
// and the tide safety card is presented as a modal
export default function RootLayout() {
  return (
    <Stack
      screenOptions={{
        // Left alone, a back button shows the previous screen's title, and under the tabs that is "(tabs)"
        headerBackTitle: 'Back',
      }}
    >
      <Stack.Screen name="(tabs)" options={{ headerShown: false }} />
      <Stack.Screen name="sites/[id]" options={{ title: 'Site' }} />
      <Stack.Screen name="tide-safety" options={{ presentation: 'modal', title: 'Tide safety' }} />
    </Stack>
  );
}

The Lesson Pages' Own Quick Quizzes

Lesson 4.1 · File-Based Routing
  1. B cleanups/[id].tsx: The folder cleanups gives the first part of the address, and the square brackets match any one segment and name it id, so e11 arrives as the id param.
  2. C ['bottom', 'left', 'right']: The header covers the top, so the screen leaves top out; the bottom still meets the home indicator, and the sides matter in landscape. Keeping top would add the status bar height again under the header on a notched iPhone.
  3. A Start npx expo start so it rewrites the route types, then run tsc again: The route types in .expo/types/router.d.ts are written by Metro. Started again, it sees the new file and adds its route; tsc then passes without any change to the code.
  4. D The string 'e08': Params come from the address, so they are text; naming the route types id as a plain string. The screen then asks the API for that id, and handles a 404 if it names no cleanup.
  5. B router.replace('/'): the list takes the dead end's place: Nothing is underneath, so canGoBack() is false and router.replace puts the Cleanups screen in place of the dead end. Back will not return to it.
Lesson 4.2 · Tabs, Nesting and Modals
  1. C src/app/(tabs)/my-cleanups.tsx, at /my-cleanups: A folder in parentheses is a group: it lets the files share a layout, the tab bar, and adds nothing to the address.
  2. A The cleanup screen belongs to the root stack, which holds the whole tab navigator as one screen underneath it: The root stack holds the whole tab navigator as one screen. The cleanup is pushed onto that stack, on top of it, so the tab bar is covered until the volunteer goes back.
  3. D ['left', 'right']: The header covers the top and the tab bar covers the bottom, each with its own inset, so only the sides are left for the screen to keep clear.
  4. B headerBackTitle: 'Back' in the root stack's screenOptions: The label falls back to the route name of the screen underneath, the tab group. headerBackTitle in the root stack's screenOptions gives every back button plain words instead.
  5. A The safety briefing a volunteer reads before a cleanup and then closes: A modal is a short detour with a clear end: read or do one thing, then close it and be back where you were. The briefing fits; lists people come back to are tabs.

Exit Ticket

  1. The file and its param: src/app/cleanups/[id].tsx; { id: 'e08' }: a string, because it comes from the address, typed from the route's name.
  2. Nothing underneath: router.replace('/'): the list takes the dead end's place. The full pattern: router.canGoBack() ? router.back() : router.replace('/') (4.1's leave(), 4.2's done()).
  3. The tree: the root Stack containing (tabs) (a Tabs navigator: Cleanups, My cleanups, Logbook, Profile), cleanups/[id], before-you-go (presented as a modal) and +not-found. The tab bar shows only inside (tabs).

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 B. Thinks in file names, not addresses. Show /_sitemap.
  • Q2 C or D. Treats generated types as something to edit or silence. Run the demo's step again and read the Did you mean.
  • Q3 A. Expects params to keep their type. An address is text.
  • Q8 B. Reads warnings as noise. Show the three-tab bar from the lesson.
  • Q10 B or C. Mixes up the three rows of the edges table: ask "which bars cover this screen?"
  • Q11 A. Thinks per tab. The cleanup must have one address for Lesson 4.4's links.

Session 11: Sign-In Flows and Deep Links

Answers and reasoning for every Session 11 item: the warm-up, both self-checks, the predict-then-check items, the lab reference outputs, the lesson pages' own quizzes and the exit ticket.

Session 11 12 self-check items Confidential

Warm-up

  1. **W1.** Which file answers the address /cleanups/e08, and how does the screen read e08? **Answer:** src/app/cleanups/[id].tsx; useLocalSearchParams<{ id: string }>() (typed params, Lesson 4.1).
  2. **W2.** The tabs live in src/app/(tabs)/. What does the folder name (tabs) add to an address? **Answer:** Nothing: a group's name is not part of the address (/profile is (tabs)/profile.tsx, Lesson 4.2).
  3. **W3.** Before you go is a modal. What does presentation: 'modal' change on a phone, and in the web preview? **Answer:** On a phone the screen slides up over the tabs; the web preview shows it as an ordinary screen with a back arrow (Lesson 4.2).
  4. **W4.** Its Done button calls router.back() only if (router.canGoBack()). Why the check? **Answer:** A modal opened straight from its address has nothing underneath: back would go nowhere, so it replaces itself with the Cleanups tab (Lesson 4.2). Today that question returns twice.

4.3 · Sign-In Flows and Protected Routes: self-check

Q1
After Theo signs in on a phone, what does Shoreline Crew write to secure storage?
A) His email and password, so it can sign in again at the next start
B) The session as JSON: the API's token and the user it belongs to
C) Only his email; the token stays in memory
D) The token, in an EXPO_PUBLIC_ variable
Why: saveCopy writes JSON.stringify(session) (token + user) under shoreline-crew-session; secure storage holds strings, so the object is saved as JSON. The password is sent once and never stored (Lesson 4.3, Never store the password).
A is wrong: A stolen password works everywhere it was reused; a token can be ended.
C is wrong: Then a restart would sign him out: the token is what must survive.
D is wrong: EXPO_PUBLIC_ text is copied into the app's JavaScript when it is built: anyone can read it.
Q2
npx tsc --noEmit prints this after a change to Profile. What is it telling you, and what are the two honest fixes?
src/app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:14:17 - error TS18047: 'session' is possibly 'null'.

14   const token = session.token;
                   ~~~~~~~


Found 1 error in src/app/(tabs)/profile.tsx:14
A guest has no session, so session can be null there: check for null first, or write session?.token when "no token" is a fine answer (as Profile does)
Why: TS18047 is TypeScript doing its job: useSession() returns Session | null. The kit ran it on the app as Lesson 4.3 ends; the output is the lesson's card, word for word.
Q3
What does expo-secure-store 58 do in the web preview?
A) It stores values in localStorage instead
B) It stores values in memory until the page reloads
C) isAvailableAsync() resolves to false, and get/set reject with a TypeError
D) It throws as soon as the module is imported
Why: The lesson measured it: isAvailableAsync() is false; getItemAsync rejects with TypeError: ExpoSecureStore.default.getValueWithKeyAsync is not a function. So loadSession() asks first and keeps the web preview's session in memory only.
A is wrong: That would be the unsafe fallback the lesson rejects on purpose: any script on the page can read localStorage.
B is wrong: The APP keeps it in memory in the web preview; the library itself stores nothing there.
D is wrong: Importing works; only the calls fail.
Q4
A teammate's sign-in screen calls setSession(session) and THEN router.back(). Theo opens it from the Profile tab and signs in. Where does he land?
A) Back on Profile, signed in
B) On the sign-in screen, which stays open
C) On the not-found screen
D) On the Cleanups tab: the guard removed the screen before back() ran
Why: The moment the session is set, Stack.Protected guard={session === null} takes the sign-in route away while it is still open, and Expo Router redirects to the anchor (the first screen). Close first, then set the session (Lesson 4.3, Step 5; measured).
A is wrong: That is what the lesson's order (back, then setSession) gives.
B is wrong: A guarded screen cannot stay: its route no longer exists.
C is wrong: The route did exist when he opened it.
Q5
request() forgets the session on a 401 only if (response.status === 401 && session !== null). Why the second condition?
A) A wrong password is also a 401, and that request carried no token: there is nothing to forget
B) Guests never get a 401
C) The API sends 401 only to signed-in requests
D) Without it, TypeScript reports that session is possibly null
Why: POST /api/auth/sign-in with a wrong password answers 401 "Email or password is incorrect." (the kit's demo). That is a failed try, not an ended session; the sign-in screen shows the API's words.
B is wrong: A guest gets a 401 from a wrong password, and from /api/auth/me ("Sign in first.").
C is wrong: The sign-in endpoint answers 401 to a guest.
D is wrong: setSession(null) compiles either way.
Q6
Why does useSession() pass a third function, () => null, to useSyncExternalStore, and what do you see without it?
The web preview draws each page on a server first, where nobody is signed in; without it Metro prints Missing getServerSnapshot, which is required for server-rendered content on every page load (phones never call it)
Why: The page still appears because React gives up on the server's version and draws it again in the browser; tsc and the linter say nothing (Lesson 4.3, the first Common Mistake; Lab 4.3's useSession.ts).

4.3 · Sign-In Flows and Protected Routes: predict, then check

P1
Predict, then check.
GET http://localhost:4000/api/auth/me
HTTP/1.1 401 Unauthorized
cache-control: no-store
content-type: application/json; charset=utf-8
vary: Origin

{
  "error": "unauthorized",
  "message": "Sign in first."
}
Why: 401 with "Sign in first.": /api/auth/me needs a token. Not "Your session has ended": no token was sent, so the client's 401 rule would not forget anything either.
P2
Predict, then check.
GET http://localhost:4000/api/sites/driftwood-cove
Authorization: Bearer st_yesterday_v04
HTTP/1.1 401 Unauthorized
cache-control: no-store
content-type: application/json; charset=utf-8
vary: Origin

{
  "error": "unauthorized",
  "message": "Your session has ended. Sign in again."
}
Why: 401 "Your session has ended. Sign in again." even for a public beach: the API checks a token before routing, because the request claimed to be someone. This is how the Cleanups list can say the sign-in has ended after an API restart.

4.4 · Deep Links: self-check

Q7
In the web preview, what does Linking.createURL('/cleanups/e08') give?
A) shorelinecrew://cleanups/e08
B) exp://192.168.1.42:8081/--/cleanups/e08
C) /cleanups/e08
D) http://localhost:8081/cleanups/e08
Why: A browser needs an ordinary web address, measured in the lesson. In Expo Go the documentation gives the exp://…/--/ form, in a build shorelinecrew://. That is why the app never writes shorelinecrew:// by hand.
A is wrong: Only a build of the app registers that scheme.
B is wrong: That is Expo Go's form, for a phone running the project.
C is wrong: A path alone is not a link anyone can tap.
Q8
npx tsc --noEmit prints this. What does the type want, and what is the fix?
src/app/cleanups/[id].tsx:97:25 - error TS2345: Argument of type '{ title: string; }' is not assignable to parameter of type 'ShareContent'.
  Property 'message' is missing in type '{ title: string; }' but required in type '{ title?: string | undefined; url?: string | undefined; message: string; }'.

97       await Share.share({ title: event.title });
                           ~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
ShareContent is "a url, or a message, or both" (two object types joined by |): give it a message (the lesson puts the link inside it)
Why: TypeScript picked the union member with message to complain about. The kit's run on the 4.4 app is the lesson's card, word for word.
Q9
You write export const unstable_settings = { anchor: 'index' } in the root layout. What do npx tsc --noEmit and npx expo lint say?
A) TS2322: 'index' is not assignable to the route names
B) Nothing new: tsc prints nothing, lint only its two env: lines
C) One lint error from expo-router's rules
D) Metro refuses to start
Why: The setting is a plain object, so no checker knows it is wrong (the kit ran both). Expo Router finds out when it builds the stack: a blank web page and "The initial route name "index" was not found in the layout", with the list of the layout's routes. Lab 4.4 Task 3 shows the same message in Jest.
A is wrong: The export is not typed against your routes.
C is wrong: No lint rule reads it.
D is wrong: Metro starts; the error appears when a page is drawn.
Q10
With the anchor in place, the not-found screen still says <Link href="/" replace>. A volunteer presses Back to the cleanups. What is on the page?
A) The Cleanups tab, exactly as before
B) A blank screen
C) Two tab bars: a copy of the tabs on top of the ones underneath
D) The not-found screen again
Why: The tabs were already underneath (the anchor); replacing the not-found screen with / put a second copy on top (measured: two tab bars). Nothing warns you. dismissTo closes screens back to the existing tabs (Step 3).
A is wrong: It looks like it, which is why this bug is sneaky.
B is wrong: Something renders: twice.
D is wrong: The not-found screen was replaced.
Q11
Which of these may a link into Shoreline Crew carry?
A) The volunteer's token, so the friend is signed in at once
B) A password-reset code
C) The id of a cleanup
D) ?join=yes, so the cleanup signs the reader up as it opens
Why: Links end up in message histories and screenshots and can be written by anyone: they carry only public ids, and a link only ever opens a screen (Lesson 4.4; Lab 4.4 Task 3 makes ?join=yes harmless).
A is wrong: Never a token or a password in a link.
B is wrong: shoreline-api has no such codes, and real ones are short-lived and single-use.
D is wrong: A link that acts on its own could be sent to anyone.
Q12
npx uri-scheme list prints Could not find any native URI schemes to list. What does it mean?
A) The project has no ios/ or android/ folders yet: Expo writes the scheme into them at build time
B) app.json has no scheme
C) uri-scheme failed to install
D) The phone is not connected
Why: uri-scheme reads the native projects, and an Expo project has none until a build (Module 9). The scheme lives in app.json until then: the kit's grep found "scheme": "shorelinecrew" in it.
B is wrong: It is there, written by create-expo-app.
C is wrong: It ran and answered.
D is wrong: list does not talk to a phone.

4.4 · Deep Links: predict, then check

P3
Predict, then check.
npx tsc --noEmit
(no output: tsc accepts it)
Why: tsc prints nothing: { message, url } is a valid ShareContent. On Android the friend gets "Join me at Weekend Cove!" WITHOUT the link (React Native's Android code passes only title and message; the lesson read it in the source, no emulator ran). Put the link inside the message.
P4
Predict, then check.
npx tsc --noEmit
npx expo lint
env: load .env.local
env: export EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL
Why: Only the two env: lines of npx expo lint; tsc prints nothing. The anchor is a plain object: Expo Router reports it when it builds the stack (the lesson's Metro card; Lab 4.4 Task 3 in Jest).

The Lesson Pages' Own Quick Quizzes

Lesson 4.3 · Sign-In Flows and Protected Routes
  1. B In secure storage, with expo-secure-store: expo-secure-store keeps it in the iOS Keychain or encrypted with the Android Keystore, where other apps cannot read it. It is async, so the app waits for it at startup.
  2. D The Cleanups tab, because the guarded route redirects to the stack's first screen: The guard is false while someone is signed in, so the sign-in route is taken out and Expo Router redirects to the root stack's anchor, the tabs, which open on Cleanups.
  3. A The API answers 401, and the app forgets the session and clears the saved copy: The API no longer knows the token and answers 401. The client forgets the session, the listener in session.ts deletes the saved copy, and the screens redraw as signed out.
  4. C Until secure storage answers, the app does not know who is signed in, and screens and guards would act on the wrong answer: Reading secure storage is async. Without the wait, the first frames would say Not signed in, and guards and redirects would act on that wrong answer before the saved session arrived.
  5. B The web preview has no secure storage, so the session lived in memory only: A browser has no keychain: isAvailableAsync() is false, so Shoreline Crew keeps the session in memory only, and a reload starts with nothing. On a phone it is saved.
Lesson 4.4 · Deep Links
  1. D exp://192.168.1.42:8081/--/cleanups/e08: Expo Go owns the exp scheme. The address says which development server to load, and everything after /--/ is the path inside your app. Linking.createURL builds exactly this in Expo Go.
  2. B The Cleanups tab appears, because the tabs were underneath the cleanup: The anchor is always at the bottom of the root stack, so the linked cleanup opens on top of the tabs, and back shows the Cleanups tab.
  3. C The Cleanups tab, because the guarded route redirects to the anchor: The sign-in route does not exist while someone is signed in, so Expo Router redirects to the stack's anchor, the tabs, which show Cleanups. A link from outside follows the same rule as a link inside the app.
  4. A "Join me at Weekend Cove!" without the link: React Native's Android code passes only the title and the message to the share sheet, so the link in url is dropped. Put the link inside the message.
  5. B Sign the volunteer up for the cleanup as soon as it opens: Anyone can write and send a link, so a link only opens a screen. Signing up, cancelling or deleting happens when the volunteer presses a button on that screen.

Exit Ticket

  1. The token: in secure storage (expo-secure-store: the Keychain, or Keystore-encrypted on Android); reading it is async, and until it answers the app cannot know who is signed in, so guards and screens would act on the wrong answer.
  2. The order: on the Cleanups tab. Setting the session made guard={session === null} false while the sign-in screen was open; Expo Router redirected to the anchor before router.back() ran. Close first, then set the session.
  3. Three forms: shorelinecrew://cleanups/e08 in a build; exp://192.168.1.42:8081/--/cleanups/e08 (their own address) in Expo Go; http://localhost:8081/cleanups/e08 in the web preview. Linking.createURL picks.

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 A. Still thinks of a session as remembered credentials. Retell the wristband: the band, never the ID card.
  • Q3 A or B. Mixes up the library and the app. The library stores nothing in a browser; the app chooses memory only.
  • Q4 A. Has not seen the guard act while a screen is open. Run Lab 4.3's last test with and without Task 1.
  • Q7 A. Expects a custom scheme to work everywhere. Point at the three-forms table.
  • Q9 A or C. Trusts the checkers with configuration. Show P4: silence.
  • Q11 D. The security point of the session: a link only opens a screen.
Instructor Kit · Answer Key

Session 12: Reaching the API and TanStack Query on Mobile

Answers and reasoning for every Session 12 item: the warm-up, both self-checks, the predict-then-check items, the lab reference outputs, the lesson pages' own quizzes and the exit ticket.

Session 12 12 self-check items Confidential

Warm-up: Session 11 Retrieval

  1. SecureStore. The session is kept encrypted on the phone and read back at startup (Lesson 4.3), so force-quitting Expo Go keeps Theo signed in. The web preview has no SecureStore, which is why that test belongs on a phone (Lesson 5.1, Step 8).
  2. 401, even for the public list of cleanups. The client forgets the session; Lesson 5.2 adds the one place that tells the volunteer ("Your sign-in has ended. Sign in again.").
  3. The cleanup's text and a link (Linking.createURL('/cleanups/ID'), an exp:// link in Expo Go) that opens the cleanup screen (Lesson 4.4).

5.1 · Reaching the API from a Phone: self-check

Q1
shoreline-api runs on your computer with plain node server.ts. Which EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL reaches it from the Android emulator?
A) http://localhost:4000
B) http://10.0.2.2:4000
C) http://127.0.0.1:4000
D) http://192.168.1.42:4000
Why: 10.0.2.2 is the emulator's built-in name for the computer it runs on (Lesson 5.1's table, from Android's documentation). Inside the emulator, localhost and 127.0.0.1 are the emulator itself.
A is wrong: localhost is the emulator, a separate virtual phone.
C is wrong: 127.0.0.1 is localhost by number: the same device.
D is wrong: Your network address works only once the API listens on it: node server.ts --host.
Q2
Predict: the API runs with plain node server.ts, and from the computer you curl its network address. What does curl say?
curl http://192.168.1.42:4000/api/health
Connection refused (curl exits with code 7): without --host the API listens on localhost only
Why: The kit ran it: curl: (7) Failed to connect to 192.168.1.42 port 4000 after 0 ms: Connection refused The API is running (localhost still answers), it just does not listen on that address. node server.ts --host listens on every address and prints the on your network: line.
Q3
What does the stage's addressAdvice return for this call (the web preview, built with the emulator's address)?
addressAdvice('http://10.0.2.2:4000', 'web')
A) Check that shoreline-api is running, then try again.
B) On Android, 10.0.2.2 is the device itself. ...
C) 10.0.2.2 works only inside the Android emulator: the web preview uses localhost.
D) Check that shoreline-api is running with --host and that this device is on the same Wi-Fi as your computer, then try again.
Why: The function checks the platform first: on 'web', 10.0.2.2 gets its own sentence. The kit ran the stage's src/api/address.ts with Node for every address and platform; this is its real output (Lesson 5.1, Exercise 1).
A is wrong: That is the web answer for localhost.
B is wrong: The "is the device itself" sentences are for localhost on a phone or emulator.
D is wrong: That is the answer for a network address on a phone.
Q4
The API answers a request sent with Origin: http://192.168.1.42:8081 (the web preview opened in a phone's browser) like this. What happens on the phone?
HTTP/1.1 200 OK
A) The cleanups load: the request reached the API
B) The API refuses the connection
C) The app falls back to localhost
D) The phone's browser blocks the answer: there is no Access-Control-Allow-Origin header
Why: The API answered (200), but it allows only http://localhost:* origins, so the browser keeps the answer from the page ("blocked by CORS policy"). Expo Go's native fetch sends no Origin and needs no header: the phone runs the app in Expo Go, the web preview stays in the computer's browser.
A is wrong: Reaching the API is not enough in a browser: CORS decides whether the page may read the answer.
B is wrong: The 200 shows the connection worked.
C is wrong: Nothing in the client does that.
Q5
.env.local says EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL=http://localhost:4000, and you start Metro like this. Which address is in the bundle?
EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL=http://10.0.2.2:4000 npx expo start --clear
A) http://10.0.2.2:4000, and Metro leaves out its env: lines
B) http://localhost:4000: the file always wins
C) Both: the phone gets one, the web preview the other
D) None: Metro refuses two values
Why: A variable set in the shell wins over the file (measured in Lesson 5.1); Metro then prints no env: load .env.local line, which is how you tell. In PowerShell the variable stays set in that window until you remove it.
B is wrong: The file is the everyday way; the shell overrides it for one session.
C is wrong: One Metro, one address: phone and web preview get bundles from the same values.
D is wrong: Nothing refuses; one value simply wins.
Q6
Why does Profile's Check the connection give up after eight seconds instead of waiting for the request to fail by itself?
A refused connection fails at once, but an address nobody answers on Wi-Fi can hang far longer; an AbortController stops the wait and the advice can show
Why: The kit's Lab 5.1 Task 3 shows both: MSW's "never answers" computer made the starter wait until Jest's own five-second limit; the solution aborts after its limit and says which address it tried.

5.1 · Reaching the API from a Phone: predict, then check

P1
Predict, then check.
curl http://192.168.1.42:4000/api/health
curl: (7) Failed to connect to 192.168.1.42 port 4000 after 0 ms: Connection refused
curl exit code: 7
Why: Refused at once: without --host the API listens on localhost only, so the network address has nobody behind it (localhost still answers). Start it with node server.ts --host. (The kit ran the API on its own port; shown here as 4000.)
P2
Predict, then check.
curl http://192.168.1.42:4000/api/sites
...
      "url": "http://192.168.1.42:4000/images/sites/pelican-point.jpg",
      "url": "http://192.168.1.42:4000/images/sites/driftwood-cove.jpg",
...
Why: Asked at the network address, every picture uses it; asked at localhost the same list says http://localhost:4000/images/… (the kit asked both). The API builds each URL from the address the request arrived at.

The CORS answers behind Self-check Q4 (three requests to /api/health: no Origin, the web preview's, the web preview opened on a phone):

no Origin                        HTTP/1.1 200 OK
Origin: http://localhost:8081    HTTP/1.1 200 OK
                                 access-control-allow-origin: http://localhost:8081
Origin: http://192.168.1.42:8081 HTTP/1.1 200 OK

addressAdvice and addressProblem from the stage (Lesson 5.1, Step 5), run with Node for every case (Exercise 1's answers are among them):

http://localhost:4000      web      Check that shoreline-api is running, then try again.
http://localhost:4000      android  On Android, localhost is the device itself. Put your computer's network address in .env.local (10.0.2.2 on the emulator).
http://localhost:4000      ios      On a phone, localhost is the phone itself (only the iOS Simulator shares your computer's). Put your computer's network address in .env.local.
http://10.0.2.2:4000       web      10.0.2.2 works only inside the Android emulator: the web preview uses localhost.
http://10.0.2.2:4000       android  Check that shoreline-api is running, then try again. (10.0.2.2 works on the emulator, not on a phone.)
http://10.0.2.2:4000       ios      10.0.2.2 works only inside the Android emulator. Put your computer's network address in .env.local.
http://192.168.1.42:4000   web      Check that shoreline-api is running (with --host for this address), then try again.
http://192.168.1.42:4000   android  Check that shoreline-api is running with --host and that this device is on the same Wi-Fi as your computer, then try again.
http://192.168.1.42:4000   ios      Check that shoreline-api is running with --host and that this device is on the same Wi-Fi as your computer, then try again.
""                               EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL is not set: copy .env.example to .env.local, then restart Metro.
"localhost:4000"                 EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL is "localhost:4000": start it with http://, as in http://192.168.1.42:4000.
"http://192.168.1.42:4000/api"   EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL is "http://192.168.1.42:4000/api": leave out the /api at the end (the app adds it).
"http://192.168.1.42:4000"       null (written correctly)

5.2 · TanStack Query on Mobile: self-check

Q7
In a browser, TanStack Query hears visibilitychange when you come back to the tab. What reports "back in the foreground" in a phone app?
A) React Native's AppState changing to 'active'
B) NetInfo
C) Expo Router's useFocusEffect
D) Expo Linking
Why: AppState.addEventListener('change', ...) reports 'active' and 'background'; useAppStateFocus passes it to focusManager.setFocused(). Without it, Lesson 5.2's quiz Q1: the list shows its cache and asks nothing.
B is wrong: NetInfo reports the connection: that feeds the onlineManager.
C is wrong: useFocusEffect is about one SCREEN coming back into view (useRefreshOnFocus).
D is wrong: Linking opens and reads URLs.
Q8
After a change to the Cleanups screen, npx tsc --noEmit starts with this. What is the fix?
src/app/(tabs)/index.tsx:34:27 - error TS2769: No overload matches this call.
src/app/(tabs)/index.tsx:91:16 - error TS2339: Property 'hasNextPage' does not exist on type 'DefinedUseQueryResult<InfiniteData<Page<CleanupEvent>, unknown>, Error>'.
A) Add hasNextPage to the query factory
B) useInfiniteQuery(upcomingEventsQuery(...)): infinite options go with the infinite hook
C) Wrap the screen in a second QueryClientProvider
D) Set "strict": false
Why: The kit made the lesson's mistake in a copy of the finished app: useQuery with an infiniteQueryOptions factory. tsc found 8 errors in the file, the lesson's card word for word; the first one is long, the TS2339 lines say the plain thing.
A is wrong: hasNextPage comes from useInfiniteQuery's result, not from the factory.
C is wrong: The provider is a runtime matter, and one is enough.
D is wrong: Hiding the types leaves the screen broken.
Q9
The phone is offline, useOnlineManager has told the onlineManager, and a screen's query needs to fetch. What is its fetchStatus?
A) 'idle'
B) 'fetching', then an error after the retries
C) 'paused': no request is sent, the cached data stays
D) 'error'
Why: With networkMode 'online' (the default) a query that cannot fetch offline pauses and continues by itself when the connection returns. Lab 5.2 Task 2 proves it headless: no request at all while offline, "Waiting for a connection…", then the first page as soon as the fake NetInfo says online.
A is wrong: idle means nothing needs fetching.
B is wrong: That is what a phone WITHOUT the online manager does.
D is wrong: fetchStatus has no 'error'; status does, and a paused query is not failed.
Q10
A test renders a screen that calls useInfiniteQuery and fails at once with this. What is missing?
No QueryClient set, use QueryClientProvider to set one
A) npx expo install @tanstack/react-query
B) A queryKey in the factory
C) The onlineManager
D) A QueryClientProvider around the screens (the root layout's job in the app)
Why: Every useQuery looks for the client the provider gives it (Lesson 5.2, Common Mistakes; Lab 5.2 Task 1's first failure). tsc and expo lint say nothing: it is a runtime rule. On a phone it should be a red error screen with the same words.
A is wrong: The import worked, or the test would fail on the import.
B is wrong: A missing key is a type error.
C is wrong: Without it the app still runs, it just never hears the connection.
Q11
Theo signs up for Boardwalk and Mudflats, then presses Sign up again from another phone. The API answers this. Why does Shoreline Crew put its invalidation in onSettled, not onSuccess?
HTTP/1.1 409 Conflict

{
  "error": "conflict",
  "message": "You are already signed up for this cleanup."
}
A) After a "no" the screens need the truth as much as after a "yes"
B) onSuccess does not exist for mutations
C) onSettled runs before the request
D) To retry the POST
Why: onSettled runs after success AND error. The 409 means the cached spots and sign-ups are out of date (this phone did not know), so ['events'] and ['signups'] are invalidated either way, and describeError shows the API's own words. Mutations are not retried; the 409 makes a repeated sign-up harmless.
B is wrong: It does; it just skips errors.
C is wrong: onMutate runs before; onSettled after.
D is wrong: Invalidation refetches queries, not the mutation.
Q12
The past-cleanups list has two pages loaded and is refetched. Which pages does TanStack Query ask for, in which order, and why does the list not shrink back to one page?
Page 1, then page 2: an infinite query refetches every loaded page in order, so the pages stay consistent and the list keeps its length
Why: Lab 5.2's last test records the requests: ['page 1', 'page 2']. Lesson 5.2 measured the same on the Cleanups tab ("A List That Pages Itself"). That is also why the screen keeps its own refreshing state: the cache refetches by itself a lot.

5.2 · TanStack Query on Mobile: predict, then check

P3
Predict, then check.
POST /api/events/e09/signups   (twice, as Theo)
HTTP/1.1 201 Created

{
  "eventId": "e09",
  "status": "confirmed"
}

HTTP/1.1 409 Conflict

{
  "error": "conflict",
  "message": "You are already signed up for this cleanup."
}
Why: First a spot (confirmed: 8 of 16 left afterwards), then 409 with the API's own words. That is what makes a repeated sign-up harmless, and why the screen invalidates in onSettled.
P4
Predict, then check.
client.refetchQueries({ queryKey: ['events', 'past'] })
['page 1', 'page 2']
Why: Every loaded page, in order, starting from the first (Lab 5.2's last test records the requests MSW answered). The list keeps its length; page 2 is asked for with page 1's answer in hand.

● Task 1: the list finds its cache and shows the first page
  No QueryClient set, use QueryClientProvider to set one
● Task 2: offline, the query waits (paused) and asks nothing; online again, it carries on
  expect(received).toBe(expected) // Object.is equality

  expect(received).toBe(expected) // Object.is equality

The solution: npm test (npm run typecheck and npm run lint print nothing after their two > lines; npx expo export --platform web bundles it too):

 PASS  src/lab.test.tsx
  ✓ Task 1: the list finds its cache and shows the first page
  ✓ Task 2: offline, the query waits (paused) and asks nothing; online again, it carries on

What each task shows: (1) QueryClientProvider is a runtime rule: no tool but a run finds it. (2) onlineManager.setEventListener((setOnline) => source((state) => setOnline(state.isConnected !== false))): the source's own "stop listening" goes back to TanStack Query; the test proves paused = no request at all (MSW's list stays empty) and the automatic continue. (3) getNextPageParam: (lastPage) => (lastPage.page < lastPage.totalPages ? lastPage.page + 1 : undefined); the last test shows a refetch asking for every loaded page in order. Common sticking points: isConnected === true (null at startup counts as offline, the first load waits); lastPage.page <= totalPages (asks for a page that does not exist).

Files: solution/src/Providers.tsx, online.ts, queries.ts; App.tsx shows the real source, connectOnlineManager(NetInfo.addEventListener), on a phone only.

The Lesson Pages' Own Quick Quizzes

Lesson 5.1 · Reaching the API from a Phone
  1. B Could not reach the server at http://localhost:4000, with advice that localhost is the device itself: On the phone, localhost is the phone itself, where no API runs. The client names the address and, on Android, explains that localhost is the device itself and suggests your computer's network address.
  2. C Without --host, the API listens only on localhost, so the network address refuses the connection: Without --host the API listens on localhost only, so a connection to the network address is refused, even from the same computer. Start it with node server.ts --host.
  3. A The old value is inside the bundle Metro already built: restart with npx expo start --clear: EXPO_PUBLIC_ values are copied into the bundle when Metro builds it. Reloading gets the same bundle; stop Metro and start it with npx expo start --clear.
  4. D CORS is a browser rule; a native app's fetch sends no Origin and ignores the header: CORS is enforced by browsers for web pages. A native app's fetch sends no Origin and does not check the answer's CORS headers, so it simply reads the answer.
  5. C http://192.168.1.42:4000/images/…, the address the request arrived at: The API builds each picture's address from the address the request arrived at, so the phone gets http://192.168.1.42:4000/images/... and the pictures load with no extra code.
Lesson 5.2 · TanStack Query on Mobile
  1. B Shows the cached cleanups and asks the API nothing: TanStack Query hears focus from browser events, which a phone does not send. Without AppState wired to focusManager, the cache never learns the app came back, so the cached list stays until something else asks.
  2. D The mutation pauses, and is sent when the connection comes back: Offline, the mutation is paused: nothing is sent, the button says Signing up and the panel says it will be sent. When NetInfo reports the connection, the paused mutation continues by itself.
  3. A The answer depends on who is signed in, so another person on the same phone must not get it from the cache: The answer depends on who asks. On a shared phone, the next person to sign in must get their own cache entry, not the previous person's fresh answer.
  4. C The screen's own refreshing state, set by the refresh the volunteer asked for: The list refetches on its own after every sign-up, when the app returns to the foreground and when the connection returns. Only a refresh the volunteer asked for should show the spinner, so the screen keeps its own refreshing state (and hides it while the refresh is paused offline).
  5. D The list as a guest, and Profile and My cleanups say his sign-in has ended: The 401 makes the client forget the session; the viewer in the key becomes guest, so the list asks again as a guest and shows the cleanups. The cache's error handler marks the sign-in as ended, so Profile and My cleanups say so.

Exit Ticket

  1. Address and --host: put the computer's network address in .env.local (Metro's exp:// line; no /api), restart with npx expo start --clear, and start the API with node server.ts --host. Then test it: curl, the phone's browser, the app.
  2. Two signals: coming back to the foreground (AppState 'active' → focusManager.setFocused) and the connection (NetInfo → onlineManager). Without them a phone app never refetches on return and fails offline instead of pausing.
  3. Invalidation: a sign-up changes the spots on the list and every cleanup screen (['events']) and your own sign-ups (['signups']); onSettled because after a 409 or a full cleanup the screens need the truth too.

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 A or C. Still pictures the emulator sharing the computer, like the Simulator. Show the table's emulator row and say "a separate virtual phone".
  • Q2 "a CORS error". Mixes up "refused" (nobody listening) with "blocked" (a browser rule). Read curl's words aloud.
  • Q4 A. Believes reaching the server is enough in a browser. Show the three CORS answers above side by side.
  • Q5 B. Thinks the file always wins: the shell overrides it for one session, and Metro's missing env: lines say so.
  • Q9 B. Describes a phone without the online manager: the difference the web preview cannot show.
  • Q11 B or D. Has not separated mutations from queries: mutations are not retried, queries are refetched by invalidation.
Instructor Kit · Answer Key

Session 13: Storage and Mobile Forms

Answers and reasoning for every Session 13 item: the warm-up, both self-checks, the predict-then-check items, Checkpoint 2's model answer, the lab reference outputs, the lesson pages' own quizzes and the exit ticket.

Session 13 12 self-check items Checkpoint 2 Confidential

Warm-up: Session 12 Retrieval

  1. The address and --host. .env.local gets the computer's network address (Metro's exp:// line, no /api), then npx expo start --clear; the API runs with node server.ts --host (Lesson 5.1).
  2. It pauses. With the onlineManager told by NetInfo, a query that needs to fetch offline sends no request, keeps its cached data and continues by itself online (Lesson 5.2).
  3. The session survived (SecureStore, Lesson 4.3); the cleanups loaded again, because the TanStack Query cache lives in memory. Lesson 5.3 starts here.

5.3 · Storing Data on the Device: self-check

Q1
Shoreline Crew wants to remember that a volunteer has already seen the "Before you go" briefing, so it is not shown again. Where should that live?
A) SecureStore: everything on the phone should be encrypted
B) AsyncStorage: a small flag, not secret, one key
C) SQLite: a table with one row
D) Only the query cache
Why: A small, non-secret setting is AsyncStorage's job (Lesson 5.3's table and Exercise 1 (b)). SecureStore is for secrets and warns about size; SQLite is for many records you search, sort or total; the query cache is gone after a force-quit.
A is wrong: SecureStore is for tokens and keys; a "seen it" flag is not secret.
C is wrong: A database for one flag is the sticky note in a ledger: it works, it is the wrong tool.
D is wrong: Memory does not survive a force-quit, so the briefing would come back every launch.
Q2
The logbook's own INSERT OR REPLACE runs twice with Theo's one sent log ("Bring in my sent logs" pressed twice). The kit then ran the totals query. What does it print?
INSERT OR REPLACE INTO sent_logs (...) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)   -- the same log, twice
SELECT COUNT(*) AS logs, ... FROM sent_logs WHERE volunteer_id = ?
A) { logs: 2, pieces: 84, bags: 2, weightKg: 5 }
B) An error: the row already exists
C) { logs: 1, pieces: 42, bags: 1, weightKg: 2.5 }
D) { logs: 0, pieces: 0, bags: 0, weightKg: 0 }
Why: The primary key is (volunteer_id, client_id), the same pair shoreline-api uses to recognize a log it already has, so the second insert REPLACES the row instead of adding one. The kit ran the stage's SQL on SQLite (built into Node 24) with Theo's real log from the API: the numbers are the lesson's "1 log in all: 42 pieces, 1 bag, 2.5 kg."
A is wrong: That is what a table without a primary key (or a JSON array) would give.
B is wrong: A plain INSERT would fail on the key; OR REPLACE is there so it does not.
D is wrong: Replacing keeps the row: nothing is deleted.
Q3
A note is put straight into the SQL text. What does SQLite answer?
db.exec(`INSERT INTO sent_logs (note) VALUES ('Theo's bag split by the stairs')`);
A) Error: near "s": syntax error
B) It saves the note without the apostrophe
C) It saves the note as written
D) SQLITE_CONSTRAINT: NOT NULL
Why: The apostrophe in Theo's ends the SQL string early, and SQLite cannot read the rest. The kit ran it on SQLite in Node; the lesson measured the same near "s": syntax error inside expo-sqlite's own wording in the web preview. The fix is a ? placeholder: runAsync('... VALUES (?)', note). The unfriendly version of the same mistake is SQL injection.
B is wrong: SQLite never edits your text: it either reads it as SQL or fails.
C is wrong: Only a ? value is stored as written.
D is wrong: SQLite never gets that far: the statement does not parse.
Q4
A query reads only the phone's database. Offline, its screen says "Opening your logbook…" until the connection returns. What fixes it?
A) retry: false
B) staleTime: 0
C) A useEffect instead of useQuery
D) networkMode: 'always' on that query
Why: Since Lesson 5.2 the cache knows when the phone is offline, and the default network mode pauses any query that would fetch. A queryFn that reads only the phone never needs the network. Checkpoint 2's Task 3b is this mistake on new code; the lesson measured the stuck screen.
A is wrong: Nothing failed: the query never ran.
B is wrong: That makes it refetch more often, still paused offline.
C is wrong: It would work, but throws away the cache's loading, error and invalidation for nothing.
Q5
Version 2 of an app adds a column to its table by changing CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS. What happens on a phone that already has version 1's database?
A) The table is rebuilt with the new column
B) The table exists, so the statement is skipped, and the first query that names the new column fails
C) SQLite adds the column by itself
D) The app deletes the old file and starts over
Why: IF NOT EXISTS answers "is there a table?", not "which version is it?". Lab 5.3 Task 3 shows the failure on a real version 1 file. A version number (PRAGMA user_version) and one step per version, in a transaction, bring old files up to date; never delete a user's data and never edit a shipped step.
A is wrong: Nothing rebuilds a table that exists.
C is wrong: SQLite only does what the SQL says.
D is wrong: Deleting a user's database is the thing the expo-sqlite documentation says never to do.
Q6
Why is the signed-in part of the Logbook screen its own component, MyLogbook, instead of more code at the top of the screen?
A mutation's result lives as long as the component that called useMutation, and a tab screen never unmounts; signing out unmounts MyLogbook, so the next person on the phone starts fresh
Why: Lesson 5.3 measured the draft: Theo brought in his logs, signed out, Amara signed in, and her empty logbook still said "Done: the server has 1 log of yours…". The query key's viewer keeps the DATA apart; only unmounting resets the mutation's state.

5.3 · Storing Data on the Device: predict, then check

P1
Predict, then check.
INSERT OR REPLACE INTO sent_logs (...) VALUES (?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?, ?)
{ logs: 1, pieces: 42, bags: 1, weightKg: 2.5 }
Why: One log: INSERT OR REPLACE with the primary key (volunteer_id, client_id) replaces the row the second time. The kit ran the stage's own SQL on SQLite (Node 24) with Theo's real log from the course API; the numbers are the lesson's "1 log in all: 42 pieces, 1 bag, 2.5 kg."
P2
Predict, then check.
INSERT INTO sent_logs (note) VALUES ('Theo's bag split by the stairs')
Error: near "s": syntax error
Why: The apostrophe ends the SQL string early. expo-sqlite reports the same SQLite words inside its own text (the lesson measured Error: Error code 1: near "s": syntax error). Use ? values.

The kit's whole SQL run (the logbook's CREATE TABLE, INSERT OR REPLACE, entries and totals queries copied from the stage's src/storage/logbook.ts, Theo's log fetched from the course API):

{ logs: 1, pieces: 42, bags: 1, weightKg: 2.5 }
[ 'Sunset Stairs Cleanup: Lots of wrappers by the stairs.' ]
Error: near "s": syntax error

5.4 · Mobile Forms: self-check

Q7
A field is written the web way, <TextInput {...register('leader')} />. Which tool tells you it does not work?
A) npx tsc --noEmit
B) npx expo lint
C) Metro, when it bundles
D) Only running it: a test or the web preview shows nothing is sent
Why: The props register returns happen to fit a TextInput's types, so tsc and expo lint are quiet (Lesson 5.4 measured it; Lab 5.4's starter type-checks its three register fields without a word). Running it shows the form never gets the text: the lab's Task 1 test fails, the lesson's web preview sent no request. Use useController or Controller.
A is wrong: It type-checks: that is why the mistake is quiet.
B is wrong: No lint rule knows register is DOM-bound.
C is wrong: Metro does not check types or behavior.
Q8
After onPress={handleSubmit(submit)} and onSubmitEditing={handleSubmit(submit)}, tsc starts like this. What is the fix?
src/app/sign-in.tsx:77:9 - error TS2322: Type '(e?: BaseSyntheticEvent<object, a...
  Types of parameters 'e' and 'e' are incompatible.
...
Found 2 errors in the same file, starting at: src/app/sign-in.tsx:77
A) const send = handleSubmit(submit), then onPress={() => send()} and onSubmitEditing={() => send()}
B) onPress={handleSubmit(submit) as never}
C) Wrap the fields in a <form>
D) Use register instead of useController
Why: handleSubmit returns a function typed for a web form event; React Native's press and keyboard events are other types. Made once and called with no arguments, it type-checks. The kit made the lesson's mistake in a copy of the finished app: two TS2322 errors, the lesson's card line for line.
B is wrong: A cast hides the error and keeps passing the wrong event.
C is wrong: There is no <form> on a phone.
D is wrong: register is the other web-only habit.
Q9
A ControlledField reads ref={field.ref}, value={field.value} and so on. npx expo lint ends like this. What is the fix?
~/shoreline/shoreline-crew/src/components/ControlledField.tsx
  29:12  error  Error: Cannot access refs during render

> 29 |       ref={field.ref}
...
✖ 4 problems (4 errors, 0 warnings)
A) Turn the rule off for the file
B) Call useController inside a useEffect
C) Take field apart where you get it: const { field: { ref, value, onChange, onBlur } } = useController(...)
D) Use a useRef per field
Why: The React Compiler's lint rules (on in the Expo template) treat an object holding a ref as a ref, so every field.x read looks like reading a ref during render. Destructured names are plain values. The kit's run on a copy of the finished app reports the lesson's four errors (its lines are two lower than the lesson's draft: the kit's copy keeps the file's comments).
A is wrong: Hiding a rule on a real component is a habit to avoid; here the fix is free.
B is wrong: Hooks cannot be called inside effects.
D is wrong: React Hook Form already keeps every field's ref; setFocus uses them.
Q10
The stage's debrisLogSchema checks a log whose weight was typed 2,5 on a phone set to German. What does the successful check give back for the weight?
debrisLogSchema.safeParse({ counts, bags: '1', weightKg: '2,5', note: ' By the stairs ' })
A) The message "Weight must be a number of kilograms from 0 to 5000."
B) "weightKg":2.5 (and the note trimmed)
C) "weightKg":"2,5"
D) "weightKg":25
Why: The rule trims, turns a comma into a point, checks the digits and converts to a number. The kit ran the stage's schema with Node: {"bags":1,"weightKg":2.5,"note":"By the stai.... A schema that accepts only 2.5 rejects what the phone's own decimal key typed.
A is wrong: That is what a point-only rule would say: the volunteer could not fix it from that keyboard.
C is wrong: z.output is a number: the transform runs on success.
D is wrong: The comma becomes a decimal point, not nothing.
Q11
Theo's log reached shoreline-api, the answer was lost, and the phone sends the SAME body again (same clientId). The kit sent it twice. What did the API answer the second time?
POST /api/events/e17/logs   (as Theo, clientId kit-demo-0001-a7f3)
HTTP/1.1 201 Created
A) HTTP/1.1 201 Created: a second log
B) HTTP/1.1 409 Conflict
C) HTTP/1.1 200 OK with the log it already has
D) HTTP/1.1 400 Bad Request
Why: The API recognizes a log by volunteer and clientId: a resend answers 200 with the saved log (the kit then asked for e17's logs: one). That is why the form makes the id ONCE, when it opens (useState(() => randomUUID())), and why Lesson 5.5 can retry safely.
A is wrong: Only a NEW clientId makes a second log: the Common Mistake of an id made on every press.
B is wrong: 409 is for a cleanup that has not happened yet ("This cleanup has not happened yet.").
D is wrong: The body is the same valid log.
Q12
The debris log's note is its last field. Why does its return key not send the log, and what sends it instead?
The note is multiline, so its return key types a new line (submitBehavior newline); the Send the log button sends
Why: A multiline field is the exception to the Next, Next, Go chain (Lesson 5.4, The Return Key). The same goes for the iPhone's number and decimal pads, which have no return key at all according to React Native's documentation: a visible button is required, not optional.

import { useQuery } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { Link } from 'expo-router';

import { sitesQuery } from '../queries';

  return (

      <Text accessibilityRole="header" style={styles.heading}>

}

labs/module-13/checkpoint-2/solution/src/SiteDetails.tsx
import { useQuery } from '@tanstack/react-query';
import { Pressable, StyleSheet, Text, View } from 'react-native';

import { describeError } from './api';
import { siteQuery } from './queries';

// Task 2: one site, with every state a screen that reads the network has
export function SiteDetails({ id }: { id: string }) {
  const site = useQuery(siteQuery(id));

  if (site.data === undefined) {
    if (site.isError) {
      return (
        <View style={styles.details}>
          <Text accessibilityRole="alert">{describeError(site.error)}</Text>
          <Pressable accessibilityRole="button" onPress={() => site.refetch()} style={styles.button}>
            <Text style={styles.buttonText}>Try again</Text>
          </Pressable>
        </View>
      );
    }
    return <Text>Loading the site…</Text>;
  }
  return (
    <View style={styles.details}>
      <Text accessibilityRole="header" style={styles.name}>
        {site.data.name}
      </Text>
      <Text>{site.data.town}</Text>
      <Text>{site.data.description}</Text>
    </View>
  );
}

const styles = StyleSheet.create({
  details: { gap: 8, padding: 16 },
  name: { fontSize: 22, fontWeight: 'bold', color: '#2a1257' },
  button: { alignSelf: 'flex-start', minHeight: 48, justifyContent: 'center', paddingHorizontal: 16, borderRadius: 8, backgroundColor: '#6534c4' },
  buttonText: { color: '#ffffff', fontWeight: 'bold' },
});
labs/module-13/checkpoint-2/solution/src/favorites.ts
// Task 3: the volunteer's favorite beaches, kept on the phone with AsyncStorage (one key, a JSON list)
import AsyncStorage from '@react-native-async-storage/async-storage';

import type { SiteSummary } from './api';

export type Favorite = Pick<SiteSummary, 'id' | 'name'>;

export const favoritesKey = 'crew-favorites';

function isFavorite(value: unknown): value is Favorite {
  return (
    typeof value === 'object' &&
    value !== null &&
    'id' in value &&
    typeof value.id === 'string' &&
    'name' in value &&
    typeof value.name === 'string'
  );
}

/** The saved favorites, checked: anything this version cannot read means no favorites. It never rejects. */
export async function readFavorites(): Promise<Favorite[]> {
  try {
    const saved = await AsyncStorage.getItem(favoritesKey);
    const value: unknown = saved === null ? [] : JSON.parse(saved);
    return Array.isArray(value) ? value.filter(isFavorite) : [];
  } catch (error) {
    console.warn('Could not read the saved favorites', error);
    return [];
  }
}
labs/module-13/checkpoint-2/solution/src/queries.ts
import { queryOptions } from '@tanstack/react-query';

import { getSite, getSites } from './api';
import { readFavorites } from './favorites';

/** Every site, for the list (given) */
export function sitesQuery() {
  return queryOptions({
    queryKey: ['sites'],
    queryFn: ({ signal }) => getSites(signal),
  });
}

/** One site: its own key, so each site is cached on its own (given) */
export function siteQuery(id: string) {
  return queryOptions({
    queryKey: ['site', id],
    queryFn: ({ signal }) => getSite(id, signal),
  });
}

/** The favorites saved on this phone: read from storage, not the network */
export function favoritesQuery() {
  return queryOptions({
    queryKey: ['favorites'],
    queryFn: readFavorites,
    // The answer is on the phone: no reason to wait for a connection ('online' would pause it)
    networkMode: 'always',
    // Only this app changes it (and would invalidate it): never stale by itself
    staleTime: Infinity,
  });
}
  • The workbook line (model): "npm run typecheck caught none of them: siteId instead of id is a valid name to ask for, /site/... is just a string, a screen without an error face and a query that waits are both valid code. Only running them showed it."
  • Common partial answers: href={/sites/${site.id}} (works, row 1: 1 point: the typed form makes a misspelled route a tsc error); an error face without Try again, or Try again that remounts instead of calling refetch() (row 2: 1); JSON.parse in a try but as Favorite[] on the result (row 3: 1); retry: false or enabled: true instead of networkMode (row 4: 0).
