9.3 · Updates and Monitoring: self-check

Q1
The kit ran npx expo-updates runtimeversion:resolve --platform android (development, then APP_VARIANT=preview) and kept only the runtimeVersion. Then it rewrote the location permission sentence and ran the preview line again. What does that tell you about the change?
29268b22f86138bf4fc840f53d9fee4a7e94525a
142af542088aaca299daa3c4fdc8d957b9068665

# after the new location sentence, APP_VARIANT=preview:
a510c337d75dda69cd1aaa023a06f0725d2b5330
A) It can go out as an update: only the text changed
B) It needs a new build: the preview fingerprint changed, so no installed preview build would accept an update made from it
C) Nothing: the fingerprint always changes on every run
D) It needs a new Expo SDK
Why: Permission sentences are written into the native project, so the fingerprint (the runtime version) changes; an update only reaches builds with exactly its runtime version. The kit's run of a comment in the Logbook screen kept the preview hash: JavaScript, an update (Lesson 9.3, "Runtime Versions").
A is wrong: Text in a SCREEN is JavaScript; a permission sentence is native.
C is wrong: Run twice on the same code it gives the same hash: the kit's two preview runs before the change matched.
D is wrong: The SDK did not change; one sentence in app.config.ts did.
Q2
A preview build made after Lesson 9.3's Steps 1 to 3 is on a tester's phone. You publish an update to its channel and runtime version. When does the tester first see the new code?
A) The moment you publish
B) When the app is next started after the update downloaded in the background (Expo's docs: sometimes up to two restarts)
C) Only after reinstalling the build
D) After the next store review
Why: The app checks at every start (ON_LOAD), never waits for the answer (launch wait 0), downloads in the background and runs the update at the next start. Check for an update shortens it for testers. The course could not run a preview build while it was checked: this follows Expo's docs, as the lesson says.
A is wrong: No phone is listening for your publish: it asks when it starts.
C is wrong: That is a build, not an update.
D is wrong: Updates do not go through store review; they must still follow the stores' rules.
Q3
CI fails on GitHub with these errors (the kit's fresh checkout, the first lines), while npx tsc --noEmit is clean on your laptop. What is the fix?
src/app/check-in/[id].tsx:51:29 - error TS2322: Type 'string | string[] | undefined' is not assignable to type 'string'.
  Type 'undefined' is not assignable to type 'string'.
...
Found 4 errors in 4 files.
A) Cast every id to string in the routes
B) Commit expo-env.d.ts and .expo/types
C) Turn strict off in tsconfig.json
D) Add npx expo customize tsconfig.json to the workflow before the type check
Why: A fresh checkout never ran npx expo start, so it has no generated route types and no expo-env.d.ts (both gitignored). The kit's run: the customize step printed two lines, then tsc was clean and all 57 tests passed (Lesson 9.3, Step 7).
A is wrong: The code is right; only the generated types are missing.
B is wrong: Generated files go stale; they are in .gitignore on purpose.
C is wrong: Hides real errors and still leaves Tally.tsx's transition types missing.
Q4
The course's stage introspects with EXUpdatesEnabled: false although expo-updates is installed. Why, and what turns it on?
          EXUpdatesEnabled: false,
          EXUpdatesRuntimeVersion: 'file:fingerprint'
No update address: easProject has no project id, so updates.url is undefined. Pasting your Expo project id (Lesson 9.2) makes the address https://u.expo.dev/<id> and turns it on
Why: Without an address the build runs its embedded code: nothing breaks. With an id, introspect shows EXUpdatesEnabled: true and EXUpdatesURL (the lesson's card, made with a made-up id). An Expo account is optional in this course.
Q5
Why does Shoreline Crew store APP_VARIANT and EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL in the preview ENVIRONMENT on EAS, when eas.json's preview profile already has them?
A) eas update does not read a build profile's env; with --environment it does not read .env.local either
B) The profile's values are secrets
C) EAS deletes eas.json after the first build
D) Because --message needs them
Why: Read in EAS CLI's source for the lesson: the profile's env exists only while a BUILD is made. Without APP_VARIANT an update gets the development fingerprint and reaches nobody (Lesson 9.3, Common Mistakes; Lab 9.3 Task 1's second test).
B is wrong: Neither value is a secret: plaintext is right.
C is wrong: eas.json stays in your repository.
D is wrong: --message only describes the update.
Q6
Why does Shoreline Crew send every unexpected error through one reportError(error, where) instead of calling a crash-reporting service in each screen?
A) Services cannot be called from screens
B) It makes the app faster
C) One setReporter call at startup can plug any service in later, and no screen changes
D) The stores require it
Why: One seam, many callers, like Lesson 5.1's single API address. Lab 9.3 Task 2 plugs a scrubbing reporter into the same shape, so no email address or sign-in key leaves the phone in a report.
A is wrong: They can; it would just tie every screen to one vendor.
B is wrong: Speed is not the reason.
D is wrong: The stores require honest privacy answers, not a code structure.

9.3 · Updates and Monitoring: predict, then check

P1
Predict, then run it.
npx expo-updates runtimeversion:resolve --platform android
29268b22f86138bf4fc840f53d9fee4a7e94525a
Why: The same as the development fingerprint (29268b22…): the user-facing version is not native, so an update still fits. A new version number is something you show people, so it usually comes with a store release anyway (the lesson's Exercise 1).
P2
Predict, then run it.
APP_VARIANT=preview EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL=http://192.168.1.50:4000 npx expo-updates runtimeversion:resolve --platform android
142af542088aaca299daa3c4fdc8d957b9068665
Why: The preview fingerprint (142af542…) again: EXPO_PUBLIC_ values are written into the JavaScript, so an update can change the API address. The variant itself changes the hash; the address does not.

9.3 · Lab: labs/module-24/l9_3-updates-and-monitoring

As shipped (npm test in starter), the failing tests:

The solution's run, and npm run check (tsc) prints nothing after its two > lines:

✔ Task 3: installs exactly what the lockfile says
✔ Task 3: writes the generated types before the type check
✔ Task 3: runs the tests once, then stops
✔ Task 1: a JavaScript fix for preview reaches only the preview build it was made for
✔ Task 1: an update published without APP_VARIANT reaches nobody
✔ Task 1: an update for the store app never reaches a tester
✔ Task 2: an email address never leaves the phone
✔ Task 2: neither does a sign-in key, even inside an address
✔ Task 2: the reporter sends a scrubbed copy and keeps the original
ℹ pass 9
ℹ fail 0

Task 1, the rule expo-updates applies:

export function takesUpdate(build: Build, update: Update): boolean {
  return (
    build.canUpdate &&
    build.channel === update.channel &&
    build.runtimeVersion === update.runtimeVersion
  );
}

Task 2, the scrubbing reporter:

const EMAIL = /[^\s@"'<>()=&?]+@[^\s@"'<>()=&?]+\.[a-z]{2,}/gi;
const BEARER = /Bearer\s+[\w.~+/=-]+/g;

export function scrub(text: string): string {
  return text.replace(EMAIL, '[email]').replace(BEARER, 'Bearer [key]');
}

// Send a copy, never the original: the app may still show or rethrow the error it caught
export function scrubbing(send: Reporter): Reporter {
  return (error, where) => {
    const clean = new Error(scrub(error.message));
    clean.name = error.name;
    send(clean, where);
  };
}

Task 3: npm ci (exactly the lockfile), npx expo customize tsconfig.json before npx tsc --noEmit (a fresh checkout has no generated types), and npm test -- --watchAll=false (the script watches). Debrief answers: Lesson 9.2's Shoreline Preview never takes an update (no expo-updates); the update made without APP_VARIANT has the development fingerprint and reaches nobody; the type-check mistake never shows on a laptop that has run npx expo start.

9.4 · The App Stores: self-check

Q7
Lesson 9.4's first search, run by the kit on the app after the lesson. How many places in Shoreline Crew send data over the network?
src/hooks/useRefreshOnFocus.ts:6: * Each time this screen comes back into view, fetch its stale answers again: the queries whose
src/api/client.ts:302:    response = await fetch(url, { ...init, headers });
src/api/client.ts:304:    // In the browser a failed fetch is a TypeError; on a phone, Expo's fetch throws its own error
src/components/UpdateStatus.tsx:8:// Which code this app runs, and (in preview and production builds) a way to fetch a new version
src/utils/photoFiles.web.ts:6:  const photo = await (await fetch(uri)).blob();
src/utils/photoFiles.web.ts:13:    return await (await fetch(uri)).blob();
A) Six
B) Three: client.ts and photoFiles.web.ts twice
C) One: client.ts line 302
D) None: these are all comments
Why: Three lines are comments; photoFiles.web.ts reads a photo the web preview already holds (a local address). Every byte the app sends goes through client.ts:302 to EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL: the start of the data inventory (Step 1).
A is wrong: grep finds the word, not requests.
B is wrong: photoFiles.web.ts fetches a local blob address, not the network.
D is wrong: client.ts:302 is real code.
Q8
Tidepool Count looks up the tide table: the app sends the beach's name, the service answers and keeps nothing. What do the privacy forms say about that request?
A) Not collected: data processed only for the moment of a request is not collected
B) Collected, linked to the user
C) Collected, not linked to the user
D) Shared with a third party
Why: Both stores' definition: "collected" = it leaves the phone AND is kept longer than the request needs (Google calls the exception "ephemeral"). Lab 9.4 Task 2's inventory has exactly this row.
B is wrong: Nothing is stored with the account.
C is wrong: Nothing is kept at all.
D is wrong: Sharing is a transfer of COLLECTED data to a third party.
Q9
You replaced com.example with your own domain in app.config.ts. The kit's run of npm test -- --watchAll=false app.config ends like this. What do you do?
  ✕ builds Shoreline Dev from the development profile
  ✕ builds Shoreline Preview from the preview profile, with an API address
  ✕ refuses the production profile until it has an https API address

  ✓ sends store uploads to testers first (Lesson 9.4)
Tests:       3 failed, 2 passed, 5 total
A) Put com.example back: the tests are right
B) Update the three expected ids in app.config.test.ts to your own
C) Delete app.config.test.ts
D) Run npx expo install --fix
Why: The tests did their job: they said exactly which ids they expected (Expected: "com.example.shorelinecrew.dev"). Change the ids ONCE, before the first upload, and the tests with them (Lesson 9.4, Common Mistakes).
A is wrong: com.example is refused by Google Play and is not yours.
C is wrong: The tests guard the profiles; keep them.
D is wrong: No package is involved.
Q10
In the production variant's introspect, what does 'tools:node': 'remove' under SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW mean, and how many tools:node lines does the PREVIEW variant print?
                  'android:name': 'android.permission.SYSTEM_ALERT_WINDOW',
                  'tools:node': 'remove'
The store build leaves the "draw over other apps" permission out (blockedPermissions, production only); preview prints 1 line (RECORD_AUDIO only), production 2
Why: Development tools may need the permission, a volunteer's app does not, and reviewers compare the permission list with what the app does (Lesson 9.4, Step 3; the kit's Predict P3).
Q11
A learner asks whether they must buy an Apple Developer Program or Google Play Console account to finish this course. What is the answer?
A) Yes: Google Play's, to pass the final project
B) Yes: both, for Lesson 9.4
C) Only an Expo paid plan
D) No: no store account and no paid developer account is needed for the course or to pass
Why: Lesson 9.4 says every step works without a store account and costs nothing; the launch-readiness check prints "Store accounts: NOT REQUIRED". An Expo account is optional too; the final project's launch row passes with a free route.
A is wrong: The final project's rubric accepts a free route.
B is wrong: The lesson's steps are written for no account.
C is wrong: Expo's Free plan covers the course; an account itself is optional.
Q12
The kit set EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL=http://crew-api.example.org (a real-looking server) and asked for the production config. The guard still refuses. Why, and what would a store version of Shoreline Crew need?
A production build needs an https:// EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL, not "http://crew-api.example.org".
The guard wants https, not just "not localhost"; a store app needs an https server every volunteer's phone can reach (the course's API runs on your computer)
Why: Lesson 9.1's guard refuses plain http for production; EAS's production profile has no address at all. That is "the honest end of this course's road" for Shoreline Crew; your own app in Lesson 10.1 can go all the way.

9.4 · Lab: labs/module-24/l9_4-the-app-stores

As shipped, the failing tests:

✖ Task 1: keywords are counted in bytes
✖ Task 1: Google Play's short description is short
✖ Task 2: what is processed only on the phone is not collected
✖ Task 2: the form lists exactly what leaves the phone and is kept
✖ Task 2: an app that creates accounts must offer deletion inside the app

The solution's run; npm run check prints nothing:

✔ Task 1: a listing within every limit has no problems
✔ Task 1: keywords are counted in bytes
✔ Task 1: Google Play's short description is short
✔ Task 2: what is processed only on the phone is not collected
✔ Task 2: the form lists exactly what leaves the phone and is kept
✔ Task 2: an app that creates accounts must offer deletion inside the app
ℹ pass 6
ℹ fail 0

Task 1: utf8Bytes = new TextEncoder().encode(text).length; Google Play's short description allows 80 characters. Task 2:

export function answerFor(item: DataItem): Answer {
  if (!item.leavesPhone || !item.kept) return 'not collected';
  return item.linkedToAccount ? 'collected, linked to the user' : 'collected, not linked to the user';
}

and deletionInApp returns app.createsAccounts. Debrief: location (worked out on the phone) and the tide-table lookup (not kept) are not collected; the crash reports are collected though not linked. Shoreline Crew has the delete button anyway: the policy must explain deletion, reviewers judge the spirit, and the first in-app sign-up makes it required.

The Lessons' Quick Quizzes

Lesson 9.3 · Updates and Monitoring
  1. C That build was made without expo-updates, so it cannot receive updates at all: expo-updates is native code, and that build was made before it was installed, so nothing in it can ask for updates. A new preview build made after Steps 1 to 3 can, and every later JavaScript change can then travel as an update.
  2. A A corrected sentence on the Before you go screen: Text on a screen is JavaScript, so it travels in the update bundle and the runtime version stays the same. Permission sentences, native packages and the icon are compiled into the build.
  3. D The update gets the development runtime version, which no preview build has, so no phone takes it: Without APP_VARIANT, app.config.ts gives the development variant, so the update gets the development fingerprint. No preview build has that runtime version, so none downloads it: published, and received by nobody. Storing APP_VARIANT in the preview environment fixes it.
  4. B EAS, which keeps the number and adds one for each production build: With the remote source EAS keeps the build numbers, ignores any in the app config, and autoIncrement adds one for every production build. The user-facing version stays yours, in app.config.ts.
  5. A Run npx expo customize tsconfig.json in the workflow before the type check: The generated route types and expo-env.d.ts are gitignored and written by npx expo start, which never runs in CI. npx expo customize tsconfig.json writes them without starting Metro, and leaves an existing tsconfig.json as it is.
Lesson 9.4 · The App Stores
  1. B Nothing: data processed only on the device is not collected: Apple only counts data that is transmitted off the device and kept: data processed only on the device is not collected. If check-in ever sends the location, that changes.
  2. D A way to start deleting the account and its data from inside the app: Both start in the app: Apple wants account deletion offered within the app (a direct link to a web page is fine when deletion finishes there), and Google wants an in-app path plus a web link entered in the Data safety form.
  3. A A rejection: the reviewer cannot sign in or see any cleanups: The reviewer's phone cannot reach your laptop, so sign-in and every list fail: an incomplete app under guideline 2.1, which asks for a demo account and a running back end. A store app needs an https server, and the production profile refuses to build without one anyway.
  4. C The latest build goes to internal testing, for the testers you invited; the listing is unchanged: eas submit uploads the latest Android build to the track the profile names: internal testing, for up to 100 testers you invite. The listing, screenshots and release notes stay as they are in the Play Console.
  5. A A closed test with at least 12 testers opted in for at least 14 days: Newer personal accounts must run a closed test with at least 12 testers opted in continuously for at least 14 days, then apply for production access. Plan those two weeks before promising a launch date, and check the page for the current rule.

Exit Ticket

  1. A screen's text: update; a permission sentence or a native package: build. Check with npx expo-updates runtimeversion:resolve --platform android with the variant set: a different hash than the build means a build.
  2. Sent off the phone and kept longer than the request needs. Shoreline Crew's location is not collected: the distance is computed on the phone.
  3. A free GitHub account for CI; an Expo account is optional; no store account and no paid developer account.

Session 25: Final Project Workshop and Real Mobile Projects

Answers and reasoning for every Session 25 item: the warm-up, both self-checks, the predict-then-check items, the lab reference outputs (including the demo runs), the lesson pages' own quizzes and the exit ticket.

Session 25 12 self-check items Confidential

Warm-up: Part 3 in Five Questions

  • W1 (Lesson 7.3 / row 7): Replace the slide with an instant change or a short fade (useReducedMotion()); motion that explains a change stays, decoration goes.
  • W2 (Lesson 8.1 / row 7): accessibilityRole="button" and an accessibilityLabel ("Delete photo"), with a target of at least 44 × 44 points (48 on Android).
  • W3 (Lessons 8.2 and 8.3 / row 8): As a plain function in Jest (no screen, no network), then one screen test with React Native Testing Library where the person sees it.
  • W4 (Lesson 9.1 / row 9): The name, id and settings of the app that is built (development, preview, production), so the three can sit side by side on one phone.
  • W5 (Lesson 9.3 / row 9): An EAS Update to the same runtime version (preview channel first); a native change would need a new build.

10.1 · Final Project: self-check

Q1
Lab 10.1: the kayak shop added 'cancelled' to RentalStatus, and npx tsc --noEmit says this. What is it telling you?
src/rentals/labels.ts:11:14 - error TS2741: Property 'cancelled' is missing in type '{ booked: string; out: string; returned: string; }' but required in type 'Record<RentalStatus, string>'.
src/rentals/labels.ts:18:14 - error TS2741: Property 'cancelled' is missing in type '{ booked: "out"[]; out: "returned"[]; returned: never[]; }' but required in type 'Record<RentalStatus, readonly RentalStatus[]>'.
Found 2 errors in the same file, starting at: src/rentals/labels.ts:11
A) RentalStatus must become an enum
B) Two Record tables keyed by RentalStatus have no entry for cancelled: each is a decision to make (its words, its moves)
C) labels.ts has a syntax error on line 10
D) Remove 'cancelled' from the union until the screens exist
Why: A Record keyed by a union must list every value, so a new status turns into a to-do list: its label, and which moves lead to it and from it. An array of statuses would have stayed silent (Lesson 10.1, Common Mistakes: adding a status without its tables).
A is wrong: An enum changes nothing: the tables still need the entry.
C is wrong: Each error is TS2741, a missing property: the file parses fine.
D is wrong: The status is the client's decision; hiding it postpones the same two decisions.
Q2
Which of Saltwick Kayak Hire's rules can only the server (the mock API) check?
A) The customer's first name is not empty
B) The return time is after the start
C) The kayak is not already booked for part of that time
D) The status is one of the four words
Why: Only the server sees every booking from every phone; it answers 409 with the message under the field. Field rules belong in the form's schema AND the API's body check, but the form can check them alone (the starter's duplicate-name rule is the same kind as the overlap).
A is wrong: A form schema checks that on the phone (and the API checks it again).
B is wrong: Also a field rule: the form can check it.
D is wrong: A type guard checks that anywhere; no other bookings are needed.
Q3
APP_VARIANT=production npx expo config in the worked example, with .env.local from .env.example. What happened, and what does the project launch with instead?
env: load .env.local
env: export EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL
Error: Error reading Expo config at ~/projects/copperline-couriers/app.config.ts:

A production build needs an https:// EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL, not "demo".
Error: A production build needs an https:// EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL, not "demo".
...
The starter's app.config.ts refuses a production build unless EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL is an https:// server: demo data cannot ship to a store. The launch uses the preview profile (demo data): an EAS internal preview build or a recording on a real phone; the release checklist's production line says "not possible yet".
Why: The guard is Lesson 9.1's, working on purpose (Lesson 10.1, Common Mistakes: trying a store build on demo data). Row 9 accepts "not possible yet: no https server" as an honest answer.
Q4
Milestone 1 is done. What does Milestone 2, the walking skeleton, ask for?
A) Every screen in the worksheet exists and is reachable, the main list and detail show the (mock) data, and the example Item is deleted
B) The device features and their permission paths
C) The accessibility pass and screen tests
D) A store build on Google Play internal testing
Why: The thinnest whole app, ugly but real, before any polish: it shows the problems that appear only when the pieces meet (a navigation shape that does not fit the brief) while they are cheap. Device features come after the main journey, on purpose.
B is wrong: That is Milestone 4: a camera button on a screen that saves nothing proves nothing.
C is wrong: That is Milestone 5.
D is wrong: Never required: store testing is only one optional route to "strong" in row 10.
Q5
A learner has an Android phone, no Apple or Google developer account, and a brief whose main journey uses the camera. Which launch meets rubric row 10?
A) Screenshots of the web preview
B) None until they pay for Google Play
C) Only TestFlight counts as shipped
D) A free EAS internal-distribution preview build on testers' phones (or a recorded demo in Expo Go on a real phone), with a README and a peer review given and received
Why: "Nothing in this rubric needs money": a preview build from an internal distribution link (a free Expo account; Android installs from the link) or a recording on a real phone is a full launch. The web preview is a launch only for an app without device features.
A is wrong: A camera journey cannot be shown in the web preview.
B is wrong: No row needs a paid account.
C is wrong: TestFlight needs the paid Apple membership and is optional.
Q6
With EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL=demo, where does the starter's data live?
A) In each phone's memory, in the mock API: two phones never see each other's changes, and it starts again when the app restarts
B) In shoreline-api on your computer
C) In AsyncStorage, kept between restarts
D) On Expo's servers
Why: client.ts calls handle() inside the app after a short pause; no server, no Wi-Fi. The first screen says so ("Demo data: changes are kept until the app restarts."). Honest for a demo and for testers, not a back end: the production guard refuses it.
B is wrong: That is Shoreline Crew's setup; the starter needs no server.
C is wrong: Nothing is stored: a restart brings the sample data back.
D is wrong: Expo never sees your app's data.

10.2 · Working on Real Mobile Projects: self-check

Q7
The check-in photo, estimated in parts: 5.5, 10 and 19 hours (the lesson's example numbers). The three-point formula gives this. What do you tell the client?
node -e "console.log((5.5 + 4 * 10 + 19) / 6)"
10.75
A) About two working days for the app, possibly three, once the server can accept the photo; the server part is not included and the store privacy answers change first
B) 10.75 hours
C) Two hours: it is one button
D) It cannot be estimated until it is finished
Why: A range, the assumptions and what you depend on (Lesson 10.2, Estimating Mobile Work). The formula's number is for you; the gap between optimistic and pessimistic is what you discuss.
B is wrong: A single number with two decimals sounds certain and hides the dependency on the server.
C is wrong: The happy path only: the server, permissions, two platforms, tests and privacy are where the time goes.
D is wrong: A spike of an hour or two, then a range, is the professional answer.
Q8
A teammate ran npm install react-native-gesture-handler@latest; the check says this. The same command runs in CI after npm ci. What happens there?
env: load .env.local
env: export EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL
The following packages should be updated for best compatibility with the installed expo version:
  react-native-gesture-handler@3.5.0 - expected version: ~3.3.0
Your project may not work correctly until you install the expected versions of the packages.
✔ Fix dependencies? … no
Found outdated dependencies
A) It waits for someone to answer the question
B) It fixes the package and passes
C) It passes with a warning
D) It prints the mismatch and exits with code 1: the pull request turns red and names the package
Why: In CI there is nobody to answer "Fix dependencies?": with CI set, npx expo install --check asks nothing, prints the mismatch and "Found outdated dependencies" and exits 1 (measured in Lesson 10.2, Exercise 3). The repair is npx expo install --fix, committed.
A is wrong: With CI set the prompt is skipped.
B is wrong: Only --fix (or answering Y) changes anything.
C is wrong: A mismatch is an error exit, which is the point of putting it in CI.
Q9
npm outdated in shoreline-crew lists TypeScript 7.0.2 as Latest. What is the right reading?
Package                                    Current  Wanted   Latest  Location                                                Depended by
typescript                                   6.0.3   6.0.3    7.0.2  node_modules/typescript                                 shoreline-crew
A) Update it now: Latest has the most fixes
B) npm outdated is broken for TypeScript
C) Leave it: SDK 58 pins TypeScript 6.0 (7.0 breaks npx expo lint, Lesson 1.3); "Latest" does not know about Expo, so wait until an SDK moves
D) Remove TypeScript and use JavaScript
Why: Expo tests each SDK with specific versions; "what npx expo install picks" is the right version for an Expo package. For plain JavaScript packages Expo does not know (Zod, TanStack Query), npm outdated is the right guide: release notes, a branch, CI.
A is wrong: The newest is not the one the SDK was tested with.
B is wrong: It reports npm's facts correctly; it simply does not know Expo's.
D is wrong: Never the answer to a version table.
Q10
Which step of the platform ladder is Shoreline Crew on?
A) Expo Go only, forever
B) Committed native folders, edited by hand
C) Continuous Native Generation: android/ and ios/ are generated from app.config.ts and plugins (by EAS Build), never edited or committed
D) Ejected
Why: You never committed native folders; EAS Build generated them from your config each time. That is the step most teams should stay on; committed native folders are a choice with a lasting cost (every SDK upgrade merged by hand).
A is wrong: Lesson 9.2 made development and preview builds.
B is wrong: There is no android/ or ios/ folder in the repository.
D is wrong: "Eject" is the old word for committing native folders once and maintaining them by hand.
Q11
The release train's cut-off was yesterday. Today a fix that changes a permission sentence in app.config.ts is merged. When can it reach users?
A) Tonight, as an EAS Update
B) On the next train: a permission sentence is native config, so it needs a new build, review and rollout
C) Right away, by editing the store listing
D) Only after the next Expo SDK
Why: Updates carry JavaScript and assets to the same runtime version; anything native (a package, a config plugin, a permission, app.config.ts) waits for a build. Ask the pull request template's question before promising a time: native change or not?
A is wrong: An update cannot change native configuration.
C is wrong: The listing is not the app.
D is wrong: SDK upgrades have nothing to do with it.
Q12
Your decision record docs/decisions/001-expo-go-or-dev-build.md has a "Revisit when" line. Why is it the most important line?
It turns the decision into a condition you check (for example "when we add a native package Expo Go does not include"), so the decision is reopened when the facts change instead of being defended or forgotten.
Why: Lesson 10.2, Step 4: decision records tell the next person (often you, a year later) why; the revisit condition says when the why stops holding.

Every output below is real: the labs run exactly as labs/check_labs.py runs them, in a terminal like a learner's, in a fresh copy of the lab folder; Lab 10.1's runs copy the course download final-project-kit first and work in its starter. Test durations are left out; everything else is as printed.

Lab 10.1 (guided): labs/module-25/l10_1-final-project/starter → solution

As shipped, copied into the starter: npx tsc --noEmit and npm test -- --watchAll=false src/rentals (verdict lines):

src/rentals/labels.ts:11:14 - error TS2741: Property 'cancelled' is missing in type '{ booked: string; out: string; returned: string; }' but required in type 'Record<RentalStatus, string>'.
src/rentals/labels.ts:18:14 - error TS2741: Property 'cancelled' is missing in type '{ booked: "out"[]; out: "returned"[]; returned: never[]; }' but required in type 'Record<RentalStatus, readonly RentalStatus[]>'.
Found 2 errors in the same file, starting at: src/rentals/labels.ts:11
 FAIL  src/rentals/labels.test.ts
 FAIL  src/rentals/rentals.test.ts
Tests:       8 failed, 1 passed, 9 total

The solution: the same two commands (tsc prints nothing; npx expo lint prints nothing), and the whole starter's suite with the lab's files in it:

 PASS  src/rentals/labels.test.ts
 PASS  src/rentals/rentals.test.ts
Tests:       9 passed, 9 total
Test Suites: 8 passed, 8 total
Tests:       31 passed, 31 total

What each task shows: (1) a new union value is a decision per Record table: cancelled gets a label and is finished, and only a booked rental can move to it (accept "out can be cancelled" only with a reason the shop would agree to, and the test changed with it); isLate changes with the clock, so it is computed; (2) the overlap rule needs every booking, so only the server (mock API) can keep it; it answers 409 with the message under start so the form shows it in the right place, and returned and cancelled rentals free the kayak; (3) the nextSteps table decides every move, so a skipped step and a finished rental are the same check; the must-never-break test names the rule. Watch for Partial<Record<…>> (it silences tsc and the tables can no longer be trusted), and for overlap checks with <= (a booking that starts when another ends is fine).

The Lesson Pages' Own Quick Quizzes

Lesson 10.1 · Final Project
  1. B The brief's types in src/api/types.ts, next to the example Item: Types first: the screens, the mock API, the tests and the offline queue all read them, and a union with Record tables makes TypeScript list every place a change must reach. Adding them next to Item keeps every check green while you build.
  2. C Keep it in a queue on the phone with a clientId, show it as not sent yet, and send it once when back online: That is Lesson 5.5's queue: the report waits on the phone with an id made on the device, the volunteer can see it has not been sent yet, and it is sent once when the phone is online again; the mock API recognizes a resend by its clientId.
  3. A A recording of the main journey in Expo Go on your own phone, with a README and a peer review: Every launch option in the rubric is free: a preview build from an internal distribution link (a free Expo account), or a recorded demo on a real phone in Expo Go or a development build. Store testing tracks are one optional way to reach strong, never required.
  4. D It stops with: A production build needs an https:// EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL, not "demo".: app.config.ts refuses a production build unless EXPO_PUBLIC_API_URL starts with https://, so demo data and plain http never reach a store build. A local-only brief changes that check on purpose.
  5. C In the mock API (a 409 for a skipped step) with a test, using the same nextSteps table the screen uses for its buttons: The server keeps the rule whatever a phone sends: the mock API answers 409 for a skipped step and a Jest test proves it. The same nextSteps table lets the screen offer only the allowed buttons, so the rule is written once.
Lesson 10.2 · Working on Real Mobile Projects
  1. D Run npx expo install --fix and commit the result: npx expo install --fix installs the version the SDK expects for every listed package, putting package.json and the lockfile back. Expo tests each SDK with specific native package versions, and Expo Go carries exactly those.
  2. A About two to three days for the app once the server accepts photos, not counting the server work, and the store privacy answers change first: A range from a task breakdown, with the assumptions and dependencies named: the server endpoint, the permission path, two platforms, tests and the changed privacy answers. That is a forecast the client can plan with.
  3. C A local native module with the Expo Modules API (Swift and Kotlin), used in a development build: The Expo Modules API lets you write a local native module in Swift and Kotlin with a TypeScript interface. It needs a development build, because Expo Go only contains the SDK's own modules; the rest of the app stays as it is.
  4. B To 57 on a branch (notes, install, --fix, doctor, checks, new builds), merge, then the same for 58: One SDK at a time, on a branch: read the release notes, install the next SDK, npx expo install --fix, npx expo-doctor, the three checks, new builds on real phones, merge with green CI. Then repeat for the next SDK.
  5. A An EAS Update: on the preview channel first, then production; the next build includes it too: A JavaScript-only fix for the same runtime version can go out as an EAS Update (Lesson 9.3): test it on the preview channel, then publish to production. The fix also goes into the next train's build.

Exit Ticket

  1. Brief and no-signal behavior: a specific person and action ("a rider marks a stop delivered, and it is sent once later"). A feature list ("offline support") is a sign the charter needs another pass.
  2. The must-never-break rule: a rule the mock API keeps whatever a phone sends ("a delivery never skips a step and never changes once finished"), tested in src/mock-api/<resource>.test.ts, named so in the test.
  3. A range: two numbers and a dependency ("two to three days, once the camera permission path from Shoreline Crew is copied in").

Common Wrong Answers and What They Reveal

  • Q1 A or D. Sees the tables as boilerplate rather than decisions. Rerun the demo's new-status step and read each error as a question for the client.
  • Q2 A or B. Mixes field rules (the form can check them) with rules that need every record. Ask: "what would the phone need to know to check it?"
  • Q5 A. Thinks the web preview is a launch for any app. Only for an app without device features.
  • Q7 B. Gives the formula's number to a client. The number is for you; the range and the dependency are for them.
  • Q9 A. Trusts npm's Latest for an Expo package. Show the SDK's version table (Lab 10.2 Task 2).
  • Q11 A. Thinks updates carry anything. A permission sentence is native configuration: a build.
Instructor Kit · Answer Key

Session 26: Final Project Showcase and Final Assessment

Answers and reasoning for every Session 26 item: the Part 3 self-check, the evidence check's reference outputs, where the written final's key is, and what to listen for in the retrospective.

Session 26 12 self-check items Confidential

Self-check: the Part 3 Review

Q1
The logbook's pieces tally counted up to 42 with withSpring, sampled every few milliseconds. Why is that a problem, and what does the lesson use instead?
0 3 8 12 19 27 30 37 42 44 47 48 49 48 47 46 45 44 43 42 41 42
A) Nothing is wrong: springs feel alive
B) The phone is too slow for 60 frames
C) A spring overshoots its target: the tally showed totals that were never true (49, then 41). Numbers and progress get withTiming
D) Use setInterval for numbers
Why: Springs are for things that move like objects; anything that must not pass its target is a timing (Lesson 7.1, Common Mistakes).
A is wrong: Alive is fine for a card, not for a count people read.
B is wrong: The samples show the spring's own curve, not dropped frames.
D is wrong: A timer on the JavaScript thread is the old, janky way.
Q2
A tutorial's Gesture.Pan().onEnd(…) was copied into the course's Gesture Handler 3 hook config. What is the fix?
src/components/SwipeToRemove.tsx:42:5 - error TS2353: Object literal may only specify known properties, and 'onEnd' does not exist in type 'PanGestureConfig'.
A) The callbacks are properties of one config object with Gesture Handler 3's names: onActivate and onDeactivate; fix the first error and the second goes
B) Downgrade Gesture Handler
C) Add // @ts-ignore
D) Wrap the component in a Pressable
Why: The second error (an implicit any) follows from the first: for a property TypeScript does not know, it cannot type the event (Lesson 7.2).
B is wrong: The SDK pins Gesture Handler 3; the old API is the tutorial's.
C is wrong: Silencing it leaves a callback that never runs.
D is wrong: A Pressable is the button behind the gesture, not a fix for its config.
Q3
With only lottie-react-native installed, the phone is happy and tsc and lint are silent, but npx expo export --platform web stops with this. What is missing?
Error: Unable to resolve module @lottiefiles/dotlottie-react from ~/shoreline/shoreline-crew/node_modules/lottie-react-native/lib/module/LottieView/index.web.js: @lottiefiles/dotlottie-react could not be found within the project or in these directories:
A) A development build
B) The web player: install @lottiefiles/dotlottie-react with npx expo install
C) A Metro config change
D) Nothing: Lottie does not work on the web
Why: lottie-react-native's web version imports the dotlottie web player; on the web it must be installed too (Lesson 7.3).
A is wrong: This is the web bundle, not a native module.
C is wrong: Metro is resolving correctly: the package is not there.
D is wrong: It works once the web player is installed.
Q4
A decorative icon is hidden from screen readers with aria-hidden="true". What does the type checker want?
src/components/Icon.tsx:35:6 - error TS2322: Type '{ name: { ios: "calendar"; android: "calendar_today"; web: "calendar_today"; } | { ios: "mappin.and.ellipse"; android: "location_on"; web: "location_on"; } | { ios: "person.2"; android: "group"; web: "group"; } | ... 7 more ... | { ...; }; size: number; tintColor: string | ... 1 more ... | NativeColorValue; "aria-hi...' is not assignable to type 'ViewProps'.
  Types of property '"aria-hidden"' are incompatible.
    Type 'string' is not assignable to type 'boolean | undefined'.
A) accessible="false"
B) A label instead
C) importantForAccessibility on iOS
D) A boolean: aria-hidden on its own (it means true) or aria-hidden={false}
Why: React Native's ARIA props are typed values, not HTML attribute strings (Lesson 8.1).
A is wrong: Also a string where a boolean belongs.
B is wrong: A decorative icon should be skipped, not named.
C is wrong: That is an Android prop, and the type error is about the value's type.
Q5
Why does the course's Jest setup put Node's own fetch back before the tests run?
A) Node's fetch is faster
B) Jest has no fetch at all
C) Expo SDK 58 replaces fetch with a phone-only one, and MSW can only answer Node's
D) To reach the real shoreline-api
Why: The custom Jest environment restores Node's fetch so MSW 3 can answer every request; an unhandled one fails the test, so no test depends on a server (Lesson 8.2).
A is wrong: Speed is not the reason.
B is wrong: There is one: Expo's, for phones.
D is wrong: The point is never to reach a server.
Q6
npx expo export --platform android in Lesson 8.4. What is this number for?
Android Bundled 19343ms node_modules/expo-router/entry.js (2084 modules)
...
› android bundles (1):
_expo/static/js/android/entry-a0fc6b8a5faced8847b7291067d2d3bd.hbc (5.5MB)
A) A measurement: write it down, change one thing (removing packages the app never uses), export again and compare
B) The store's size limit
C) How much memory the app uses
D) Nothing: bundle sizes do not matter on phones
Why: Measure before you optimize, and after: the rubric's row 8 asks for one measurement written down, and "strong" for one that led to a change.
B is wrong: It is the JavaScript bundle, not the store package.
C is wrong: Memory is measured on a device, not by the export.
D is wrong: A bigger bundle takes longer to load and parse on every start.
Q7
npx expo-doctor before a build. What does it want, and why?
19/20 checks passed. 1 checks failed. Possible issues detected:
✖ Check that no duplicate dependencies are installed
Found duplicates for expo-server:
  ├─ expo-server@58.1.0 (at: node_modules/expo-server)
  └─ expo-server@57.0.3 (at: node_modules/expo-atlas/node_modules/expo-server)
A) Delete node_modules and reinstall
B) Ignore it: only one check failed
C) Pin expo-atlas to the newest version
D) One copy of each native module: an overrides entry in package.json tells npm which expo-server the other package gets
Why: Native builds contain one version of each native module; two copies can lead to unexpected build errors. The older copy came with a development tool (Lesson 9.1).
A is wrong: npm would install the same two copies again.
B is wrong: It is the check that protects the build.
C is wrong: Its newest version may still ask for the previous SDK's expo-server.
Q8
When does a project need a development build instead of Expo Go?
A) As soon as it has more than five screens
B) When it needs a native package Expo Go does not contain, its own name, icon and ids on the phone, or a config plugin's settings
C) Only for the App Store
D) Never: Expo Go runs everything
Why: Expo Go carries only the SDK's modules and Expo's identity; a development build is the app's own, with Metro and Fast Refresh as before (Lesson 9.2).
A is wrong: Screens are JavaScript: Expo Go runs any number.
C is wrong: Builds are about native code, not stores.
D is wrong: Only the modules inside Expo Go.
Q9
The update settings inside a build. What does file:fingerprint mean for an update after a native change?
EXUpdatesRuntimeVersion: 'file:fingerprint'
A) Updates ignore runtime versions
B) The fingerprint is a password for updates
C) The runtime version is a fingerprint of the native side: a native change gives a new runtime version, so it needs a new build; updates reach only builds with the same fingerprint
D) Every update makes a new fingerprint
Why: That is what keeps an update from loading JavaScript on a build whose native code does not match it (Lesson 9.3).
A is wrong: They match on it exactly.
B is wrong: It is a hash of the native project, not a secret.
D is wrong: JavaScript-only updates keep the fingerprint.
Q10
The ids were changed to the client's own domain (org.marisolcrew) and the app config test fails. What is the test protecting, and what do you do?
 FAIL  ./app.config.test.ts
  ✕ builds Shoreline Dev from the development profile (186 ms)
  ✕ builds Shoreline Preview from the preview profile, with an API address (1 ms)
  ✕ refuses the production profile until it has an https API address (2 ms)
The app ids (permanent once a store has seen them): the test makes an id change a deliberate decision. Change the expected names and ids in app.config.test.ts in the same commit, once, before the first store build.
Why: Lesson 9.4: the ids belong to a domain the client controls; the test fails on purpose until the change is written down twice.
Q11
The evidence check on a final project ends like this. What does it mean?
4 passed, 9 to fix, 3 to check
NOT READY: fix the FIX lines above, then run this check again
A) The project failed the course
B) Evidence the rubric asks for is missing: fix every FIX line and run the check again; it lists evidence, it never scores
C) The tests failed
D) The project cannot be shown in the showcase
Why: The check reads files only (names, ids, the worksheet, the example Item, the tests, the README, the review). The rubric scores, with a person; a CHECK line is something to look at, a FIX line evidence to add.
A is wrong: It is not a score, and "not yet" rows are a to-do list.
C is wrong: It never runs the tests: CI does.
D is wrong: Every learner shows their project; missing evidence is fixed by the evidence date.
Q12
Your peer reviewer writes: "At the largest text size the late list cuts off the kayak names." How do you answer the point?
With a fix and its commit (the row wraps; a screen test at the largest font scale), or with a sentence on why you are leaving it. Every point of the review gets an answer (rubric row 10).
Why: peer-review.md ends with exactly that rule for the author; a review answered point by point is evidence for rows 7 and 10.

Lab Reference: the Evidence Check

labs/module-26/showcase-preflight/evidence_check.mjs

A tool, not an exercise. As labs/check_labs.py runs it: on the final-project starter exactly as downloaded (the course download copied into the run), the whole output:

FIX   [row 9] package.json still says "name": "starter": give the project its own name
FIX   [row 9] app.config.ts: the variants are still called Starter Dev, Starter Preview and Starter

23 passed, 0 to fix, 0 to check
READY FOR THE SHOWCASE

The fixture's own rules (Lab 10.1's solution) still pass inside the starter, as npm test -- --watchAll=false src/rentals there:

 PASS  src/rentals/labels.test.ts
 PASS  src/rentals/rentals.test.ts
Test Suites: 2 passed, 2 total
Tests:       9 passed, 9 total

Reading it: the check reads files only (no network, no install, nothing changed). FIX = evidence the rubric needs that is not there (NOT READY, exit code 1); CHECK = look at it yourself (READY WITH NOTES); each line names its rubric row. A learner with a CHECK line they keep brings a sentence for it. It never runs the tests or sees the phone: CI and the demo do.

Written Final Assessment

The 24 items and their key are in final-assessment.html, final project section ("Written final assessment: learner pages" and "answer key"): 27 points, pass mark: 19 (19 of 27, 70 %). Course complete = the final project complete under Lesson 10.1's rubric AND the written final passed.

Course Retrospective: What to Listen For

  • R1 and R2: a change from a wish ("I want to make apps") to a claim with evidence ("I built and shipped a phone app for a client, with tests and a privacy inventory"). Ask for the evidence behind each word.
  • R3: the hardest lessons are usually 5.5 (offline) and 8.3 (screen tests); what made them click is usually a red screen read from the top, or a test that failed for a real bug.
  • R4: one direction, not five; a first project that fits a month.
